[[sec-157-smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test]]
===== 157 `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test`

*BFM / Model*: SPI pad BFM, ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Run the UART/log-engine representative CSR precheck.

*Test Coverage*:

* UART/SPI/log-engine representative CSR precheck.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.


*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-UART-LOG-COUNT` |Named compare in `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-UART-LOG-LOG_ENGINE_CTRL` |Named compare in `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-UART-LOG-LOG_ENGINE_INTR_STATUS` |Named compare in `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-UART-LOG-UART_IIR` |Named compare in `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-UART-LOG-UART_LOG_ENGINE_CTRL` |Named compare in `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-UART-LOG-UART_LSR` |Named compare in `smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`; the run fails if it mismatches
|`CHK-UART-MSR-CTS-PAD0` |After this test drives UART0 CTS pad 14 to 0, a bounded poll of the CTS and DCTS bits of `UART_MSR` reads either the pre-change word or exactly the transition word CTS=1 (the inverse of `cts_ni`) with DCTS=1 for the change since the last read, and the transition word arrives within the bound (`smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-UART-MSR-CTS-PAD1` |After this test drives UART0 CTS pad 14 to 1, the same bounded poll of the CTS and DCTS bits reads the pre-change word or exactly CTS=0 with DCTS=1, and the transition word arrives within the bound (`smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-UART-MSR-DELTA-CLEAR` |The CTS and DCTS bits of the second back-to-back `UART_MSR` read equal exactly 0x10: CTS still asserted with DCTS cleared by the preceding read, as the delta-since-last-read contract in `uart_16550_main.rdl` requires (`smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`). Every MSR compare in this card is masked to CTS and DCTS, the two bits this test drives through pad 14; DSR, RI, DCD and their delta bits are logged, not compared, because the integrator pin table (`ocah_gpio_table.csv`) pins no pad for them and the bench has no specification for their level
|`CHK-UART-MSR-DELTA-CLEAR-PAD0` |The CTS and DCTS bits of the read after the pad-0 transition read equal exactly the settled word: DCTS cleared, CTS 1 (`smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-UART-MSR-DELTA-CLEAR-PAD1` |The CTS and DCTS bits of the read after the pad-1 transition read equal exactly the settled word: DCTS cleared, CTS 0 (`smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`)
|`CHK-UART-MSR-FIRST` |The CTS and DCTS bits of the first `UART_MSR` read of the run equal exactly DCTS OR CTS (0x11): CTS=1 because this test drives pad 14 low, DCTS=1 because it is the first read (`smc_uart_spi_log_engine_test_seq.py`). DSR, RI, DCD and their delta bits are outside the compare (see `CHK-UART-MSR-DELTA-CLEAR`)
|===


*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-UART-LOG-COUNT`, `CHK-UART-LOG-LOG_ENGINE_CTRL`, `CHK-UART-LOG-LOG_ENGINE_INTR_STATUS`, `CHK-UART-LOG-UART_IIR`, `CHK-UART-LOG-UART_LOG_ENGINE_CTRL`, `CHK-UART-LOG-UART_LSR` (and 6 more) hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.
'''''

[[sec-164-smc_log_engine_intr_mask_test]]
===== 164 `smc_log_engine_intr_mask_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_log_engine_intr_mask_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); observation on `tb_uart_irq_combined[0]`

*Objective*: INTR_ENABLE must mask the log-engine interrupt output, not its set path.

*Test Coverage*:

* Both consequences of the placement: an interrupt held after the enable is cleared, and an interrupt dropped because it arrived while masked.
* `INTR_TEST` is `sw = w, singlepulse`, so the stimulus is a one-cycle event on the same line as the real event and needs no bus traffic.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Ungate the UART peripheral clock and require instance 0's PLIC bit to read 0, so nothing below can be credited to the 16550 IRQ or the UART error line.
. Set INTR_ENABLE, pulse INTR_TEST, require INTR_STATUS to latch and the IRQ to assert, held 32 cycles.
. Clear INTR_ENABLE only and sample the IRQ.
. W1C the status, prove it cleared, then pulse INTR_TEST with INTR_ENABLE=0 and set the enable afterwards.
. Require INTR_STATUS to hold the event.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-MASK-ARM` |The set path, the status register and the probe are all alive
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-MASK-DEASSERT` |Clearing the enable de-asserts the IRQ, holds it de-asserted for the same span the arm leg holds it asserted, and leaves INTR_STATUS still carrying the bit -- the last is what separates an output mask from a DUT that simply clears the status
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-MASK-LATCH` |An event arriving while masked is still latched and surfaces once the enable is set
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-MASK-ARM`, `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-MASK-DEASSERT` and `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-MASK-LATCH` hold after their compares; the last two are measured before either raises, so neither hides the other. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `uart`.

'''''

==== OCCP

[[sec-158-smc_dual_elaboration_test]]
===== 158 `smc_dual_elaboration_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_dual_elaboration_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcDualHarness` (hierarchical probes on both instances); production boot ROM loaded into both

*Objective*: Prove the two-instance model elaborates and both instances leave reset, sense their fuses and finish memory init from the shared cold reset. The two reset trees are proven wired to both instances, not to be independent domains: one `rst_cold_ni` drives both.

*Test Coverage*:

* Two `smc_wrapper` instances built with `SMC_DUAL`, plus the I3C bus they share.
* Reset release, fuse sense and memory init observed on each instance, after the one cold reset both share.
* The testbench's own per-channel I3C counter indexing.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Elaborate the `dual` target with `+rom_bin64=prod_rom.bin64` on both instances.
. Read `tb_i3c_channel_id_N` and confirm cocotb indexes the flat counters the way the TB exports them.
. Wait for `powergood_stable` and primary SMC reset release on each instance.
. Wait for fuse-sense done on each instance within 200000 `clk_smc_i` cycles.
. Confirm `init_mem_done` stays low on both while `boot_stall` is held, then release and confirm it asserts.
. Sample the shared I3C0 wired-AND and confirm it resolves to idle high with nothing driving.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DUAL-I3C-INDEXING` |The channel-id ports read back as the TB exports them, so the counters are not all element 0
|`CHK-DUAL-ELAB` |Both instances elaborated and report `powergood_stable`
|`CHK-DUAL-RESET` |`rst_primary_smc_clk_no` deasserts on both instances
|`CHK-DUAL-FUSE-SENSE` |Fuse sense completes on each instance within the 200000-cycle bound
|`CHK-DUAL-BOOT-STALL` |`init_mem_done` is low on both instances while `boot_stall` is held
|`CHK-DUAL-MEM-INIT` |`init_mem_done` asserts on both instances after `boot_stall` is released
|`CHK-DUAL-I3C-IDLE` |The shared I3C0 wired-AND resolves, and resolves high, with no driver
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-DUAL-I3C-INDEXING`, `CHK-DUAL-ELAB`, `CHK-DUAL-RESET`, `CHK-DUAL-FUSE-SENSE`, `CHK-DUAL-BOOT-STALL`, `CHK-DUAL-MEM-INIT` (and 1 more) hold after their compares; a bound expiry or an unresolvable sample raises. Enrolled in: `all`, `dual`.

'''''

[[sec-159-smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test]]
===== 159 `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcDualHarness` with the inbound AXI manager (`bfm_axi`, SEP_IN); production boot ROM on both instances

*Objective*: Measure that an inbound AXI manager reaches the CPU scratch SRAM window, and hold the backdoor bank/entry decode against it.

*Test Coverage*:

* Front-door AXI write and readback into the CPU scratch banks, which is how the OCCP boot flow stages its payload.
* Six distinct patterns, placed one per field of the scratch decode: the two stripe bits (`address[7:6]`), the wrap of the four-bank cycle at +0x100, the entry low bits (`address[5:3]`), and the next 128 KB group (`address[19:17]`).
* `smc_scratch_map_pkg`, the decode the `+smc_scratch_ram_hex` image loader in `smc_cpu_mem_dv.sv` uses to scatter firmware across the 32 banks. Its geometry comes from `spm_memory.rdl` and `cpu.adoc`; the bank interleave is a DV-owned table declared in the package header, and this testcase is where that table is measured against the design rather than assumed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring both instances up with `boot_stall` released, so the CPU cluster front port answers.
. Require `init_mem_done_o` to reach 1 on both instances within the watch bound.
. Read each probe address before its write; the banks are not auto-initialised, so the pre-write value is reported and not asserted on.
. Write each of the six patterns over `bfm_axi` and read the address back.
. Require every readback to equal exactly the pattern written.
. Peek the same offset through the hierarchical backdoor and require the macro word it resolves to hold that same pattern.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AXI-INIT-MEM-DONE` |`init_mem_done_o` is 1 on both instances within the watch bound after `boot_stall` release; a flag still 0 at the bound raises
|`CHK-AXI-SCRATCH-REACHABLE` |Every pattern read back exactly as written, including the ones crossing a bank stripe and a group boundary, so neither a stuck default subordinate nor a single-bank decode can satisfy it
|`CHK-SCRATCH-BACKDOOR-DECODE` |For every offset, the macro word `smc_scratch_map_pkg` resolves to holds what AXI wrote. Checked only where the AXI readback already matched, so a decode complaint cannot be raised about a write that never took
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every pattern reads back exactly as written and the backdoor resolves to the same macro word; any mismatch raises. The +0x100 pattern carries the weight: it is the first offset at which a flat 32-bank round-robin model parts from the cluster's four-bank-per-group decode, so a backdoor built on the wrong model agrees with AXI below it and disagrees from there on. `init_mem_done_o` must be 1 on both instances within the watch bound before the first probe access; the watcher raises otherwise. Enrolled in: `all`, `dual`.

'''''

[[sec-160-smc_occp_sanity_test]]
===== 160 `smc_occp_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_occp_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: no model on either side -- `u_dut` runs the production boot ROM as OCCP target, `u_bfm` runs the `occp_sanity` DV ROM image as OCCP controller

*Objective*: One SMC issues OCCP GET_VERSION at another and reports through an OCCP WRITE.

*Test Coverage*:

* I3C bring-up on the controller: target-ready pad, ENTDAA, channel selection.
* OCCP `GET_VERSION` and a 4-byte OCCP `WRITE`, serviced by the target's ROM.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Elaborate the `dual` target with the production ROM on `u_dut` and `occp_sanity.rom.hex` applied to `u_bfm`.
. Let the controller firmware bring up I3C, issue `GET_VERSION` and check the answer is `0x1`.
. Let it report by OCCP-writing its pass/fail code into the target's scratch 0.
. Poll both instances' scratch 0: fail on `TEST_FAIL` from either, pass on `TEST_PASS` from either.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OCCP-SANITY` |Target and controller scratch 0, sampled together; `TEST_FAIL` from either fails the test
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `TEST_PASS` reaches a scratch 0 and neither instance reports `TEST_FAIL`. `CHK-OCCP-JUMP-EXECUTED` and `CHK-OCCP-CONTROLLER-NO-FAIL` are *not* carried over from the boot test: nothing executes on the target here, and `occp_sanity/main.c` never writes its own scratch 0, so both would be checks that cannot fail. The firmware consoles, I3C counters and POST code are diagnostic only. Enrolled in: `all`, `dual`.

'''''



==== FIRMWARE

[[sec-166-smc_fw_hello_world_test]]
===== 166 `smc_fw_hello_world_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_hello_world_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/hello_world`, executed by the CPU cluster; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: Boot the smallest SMC C image and read the verdict it posts over MMIO.

*Test Coverage*:

* The firmware path end to end, and nothing else: c_compile, output staging by basename, the scratch load through `smc_scratch_map_pkg`, the reset-vector handoff, crt0 and picolibc init across four harts, and the MMIO store to CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0.
* The image is `test_pass(0)` and a `wfi` loop, so no DUT feature is under test.
* Not a duplicate of `smc_cpu_firmware_boot_test`, and the two cannot be merged. That one boots `min_pass.rom.hex` from the ROM window -- a four-line hand-assembled stub that avoids crt0 and the hart barrier -- and its `CHK-ROM-IS-TARGET` requires that no scratch image be supplied. This one requires one. So they cover the ROM path and the c_compile-plus-scratch path respectively, and share no CHK token.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `hello_world` through `[c_build.default]` and stage `hello_world.ecc.hex` beside the simulator.
. Hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, so a residual verdict from an earlier test cannot be counted.
. Program the reset vector, release all four cores, then release `boot_stall`.
. Poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic, failing on `TEST_FAIL`, on the `0xBAD0_xxxx` namespace, and on the bound expiring.
. Compare the per-bank scratch read counters (`tb_cpu_scratch_bank_read_count`) snapshotted before step 2 and after step 4: every bank whose counter advanced is one of banks 0-3, and at least one did.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-HELLO-WORLD-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start, and every scratch bank the CPU read between the two counter snapshots is one of banks 0-3
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the PASS magic is read back. The image is resident in scratch banks 0-3 only, and that bank distribution is asserted from the per-bank scratch read counters: the `hello_world` link map ends at offset `0x8730` (code, data, four 4 KB stacks and the 2 KB heap), inside the first 128 KB group, which the `smc_scratch_map_pkg` decode places in banks 0-3, and an image spread across all 32 banks would mean the loader disagrees with the cluster and the CPU is fetching where the image was never written. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. On failure the report carries per-hart PCs, per-hart mcause/mepc, and a comparison of the image reachable over AXI against the built image, which is what distinguishes a mis-loaded image from a trapping one. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-167-smc_fw_hello_world_multicore_test]]
===== 167 `smc_fw_hello_world_multicore_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_hello_world_multicore_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/hello_world_multicore`, executed by all four harts of the CPU cluster; verdict and hart markers read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: All four harts of the CPU cluster run the scratch image and check in with each other; `smc_fw_hello_world_test` proves hart 0 only.

*Test Coverage*:

* The three secondary harts past the crt0 barrier. Hart 0 spins until harts 1..3 have each added to a shared atomic counter and only then posts PASS under a Freedom-Metal lock, so the verdict word itself depends on three harts running `main`. `smc_fw_hello_world_test` cannot tell a hart that stalls after `__metal_synchronize_harts` from one that runs.
* Per-hart stores the bench can attribute: every secondary hart writes its hart id into SCRATCH[hartid] before checking in, and the bench reads SCRATCH_2 and SCRATCH_3 back -- words hart 0 never writes.
* SCRATCH_1 is not asserted: hart 0 rewrites it in its wait loop while hart 1 is storing to it, so its final value is a race the image does not define. It is logged only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `hello_world_multicore` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `hello_world_multicore.ecc.hex` beside the simulator, hold `boot_stall` from t=0 (`+smc_hold_cpu_boot`), clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back.
. Clear SCRATCH_2, which doubles as the console/error word and could hold a residue from an earlier test. SCRATCH_3 is the seed word the boot contract overwrites with this run's `RANDOM_SEED` before release, so a 3 read back afterwards can only have come from hart 3; a run whose seed is itself 3 could not tell the two apart, and the sequence refuses it before release.
. Program the reset vector, release all four cores, then release `boot_stall`.
. Poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic, failing on `TEST_FAIL`, on the `0xBAD0_xxxx` namespace, and on the bound expiring.
. Read SCRATCH_2 and SCRATCH_3 over SEP_IN AXI and require the hart ids 2 and 3.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-MULTICORE-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start, with the scratch fetch evidence the boot contract carries. Hart 0 posts it only after three check-ins, so the word is a four-hart result
|`CHK-FW-MULTICORE-HART-MARKERS` |SCRATCH_2 reads 2 and SCRATCH_3 reads 3 after PASS. Neither can be a residue: one was cleared by the bench before release and the other overwritten by the seed publish
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-168-smc_fw_octs_p0_primary_test]]
===== 168 `smc_fw_octs_p0_primary_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_octs_p0_primary_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/octs_p0_primary_test`, executed by the CPU cluster; timer CSRs read over SEP_IN AXI, credit pulses counted on `tb_octs_cnt_credit_from_dut` (pad 56)

*Objective*: The OCTS system timer is configured and swept from the hart that owns it in the product: CTRL writability, TIMER_GPIO_ENABLE, 64-bit PRESET, TIMER_START, monotonic COUNT.

*Test Coverage*:

* CPU-side programming of OCTS. The cocotb OCTS leaves drive the same registers from SEP_IN; this image probes CTRL against a value that differs from the reset default in every field, programs CTRL and TIMER_GPIO_ENABLE, writes and reads back PRESET including a 64-bit value only a live HI half can return, starts the timer and requires COUNT to grow across three reads. Those compares are the firmware's.
* The timer left running after the verdict, which the bench measures on its own: STATUS.RUNNING, TIMER_COUNT_LO advancing, and credit pulses on pad 56 -- the PRIMARY outbound observation `smc_octs_dual_sync_test` uses. `tb_chiplet_is_primary` keeps its default of 1.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `octs_p0_primary_test` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `octs_p0_primary_test.ecc.hex`, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic. The image parks with the timer running and the pad output enabled.
. Read STATUS.RUNNING and TIMER_GPIO_ENABLE and require both set; read TIMER_COUNT_LO, wait 256 `clk_smc_i`, read it again and require it to have advanced.
. Count rising edges on `tb_octs_cnt_credit_from_dut` over 1024 `clk_smc_i` and require at least two.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-OCTS-PRIMARY-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-OCTS-COUNT-ADVANCES` |STATUS.RUNNING and TIMER_GPIO_ENABLE read set and TIMER_COUNT_LO advanced across 256 `clk_smc_i`, so the timer the image started is still counting when the bench looks
|`CHK-FW-OCTS-CREDIT-PAD` |At least two rising edges on pad 56 in 1024 `clk_smc_i`. Two is the floor `smc_octs_dual_sync_test` uses for PRIMARY outbound; the RDL gives CREDIT_VAL no period semantics a tighter floor could be derived from, and the image's CREDIT_VAL of 0xA pulses more often than that test's 0x10
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-169-smc_fw_i2c_sanity_test]]
===== 169 `smc_fw_i2c_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_i2c_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/i2c_sanity`, executed by the CPU cluster, I2C_0 as controller against I2C_1 as target on the shared pad bus; `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor` decoding `tb_i2c0_scl/sda`; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: I2C controller bring-up from the CPU, the `i2c_sanity` image: TIMING0-4 read-back, FMT reset on a non-empty FIFO, a 3-byte write with STOP received by the I2C_1 target, the CMD_COMPLETE lifecycle through the PLIC pending bit, CONTROLLER_HALT provoked by a NACK.

*Test Coverage*:

* What the firmware grades from both instances' CSRs: TIMING0-4 read back field by field, FMT reset on a FIFO observed non-empty, a 3-byte write whose bytes the target ACQ FIFO returns, CMD_COMPLETE read through the PLIC pending bit, and CONTROLLER_HALT at the unanswered address 0x55. The FIFO-level and byte compares are the firmware's, graded from both instances' CSRs.
* The wire, which the bench vouches for. With `+smc_i2c_shared_bus` the I2C0/1/2 pads share one resolved open-drain bus, so the transfer the firmware describes has to appear on `tb_i2c0_scl/sda` as START, address, direction, acknowledge, data bytes, STOP. A PASS word without that traffic -- an image that short-circuited, a pad mux left disabled, a target that never acknowledged -- fails here.
* Floors, not exact counts: the driver splits long transfers and retries are legal, so the wire can carry more frames than the minimum, never fewer. For a write the bytes counted are those the target acknowledged.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `i2c_sanity` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `i2c_sanity.ecc.hex`, select the shared pad bus, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, and arm `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor` on `tb_i2c0_scl/sda`.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Grade the decoded frames against the floors the image implies.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-I2C-SANITY-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-I2C-WIRE-TRAFFIC` |Emitted once per floor, twice here: at least one acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 3 data bytes (the write with STOP), and at least one frame to 0x55 whose address byte no target acknowledged (the halt provocation). Decoded passively from the pads, not reported by the firmware
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the boot token and both wire floors hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-170-smc_fw_i2c_target_sanity_test]]
===== 170 `smc_fw_i2c_target_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_i2c_target_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/i2c_target_sanity`, executed by the CPU cluster, I2C_0 as controller against I2C_1 as target on the shared pad bus; `SmcI2cMasterVip` as the external host for step S6, played from a `smc_fw_scratch_marker_watch` on SCRATCH_1; `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor`; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: I2C target bring-up from the CPU, the `i2c_target_sanity` image: ADDR0 match and mismatch, dual address, ACQ write, TX read, TX stretch control -- and the UNEXP_STOP lifecycle with the bench as the external host.

*Test Coverage*:

* Steps S1..S5, CPU-driven and graded by the firmware from both instances' CSRs.
* Step S6 needs a host the OpenTitan controller cannot be: one that ends a read with a STOP and no NACK. The image disables I2C_0, publishes 0xEBEDEBE3 in SCRATCH_1 and waits for INTR_STATE.UNEXP_STOP. The bench is that host: START, 0x10 with the read bit, one data byte, then the STOP inside that byte's acknowledge clock -- the ACK level driven, SCL allowed to rise, SDA released while SCL is still high. That is the only placement that is a STOP without a preceding NACK: once the acknowledge clock completes the target is already presenting its next bit, so a host pull on SDA is a lost transfer to it rather than an unexpected STOP, and a host that acknowledges every byte ends against an empty TX FIFO with the target stretching SCL (INTR_STATE.TX_STRETCH).
* The byte the host reads is data the DUT clocked out: the image preloads 0x77, 0x88 and the host must read 0x77. The firmware's UNEXP_STOP check is the other half of the same event.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `i2c_target_sanity` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `i2c_target_sanity.ecc.hex`, select the shared pad bus, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, arm the wire monitor and the SCRATCH_1 watcher.
. Program the reset vector and release. The image runs S1..S5 between I2C_0 and I2C_1.
. When SCRATCH_1 reads 0xEBEDEBE3, drive the one-byte read from 0x10 with the STOP inside the acknowledge clock; require the address acknowledged and the byte equal to 0x77.
. Poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic, then grade the wire: at least two frames to 0x10 and at least one to 0x20.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-I2C-TARGET-SANITY-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-I2C-TARGET-VIP-READ` |The bench host's read of 0x10 was acknowledged and returned 0x77, the first preloaded TX byte, clocked out onto the wire by the DUT target
|`CHK-FW-I2C-WIRE-TRAFFIC` |At least two frames to 0x10 and at least one to 0x20 (the dual address), decoded from the pads
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold. The image cannot reach PASS without the S6 STOP, so the VIP read precedes the verdict. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-171-smc_fw_i2c_acq_fifo_stretch_reset_test]]
===== 171 `smc_fw_i2c_acq_fifo_stretch_reset_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_i2c_acq_fifo_stretch_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/i2c_acq_fifo_stretch_reset`, executed by the CPU cluster, I2C_1 as controller into I2C_0 as target on the shared pad bus; `smc_fw_scratch_marker_watch` sampling `tb_i2c0_scl_dut_low` at two markers; `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor`; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: The target's ACQ FIFO stretches SCL automatically when full, and ACQ reset releases the stretch, observed on the target's own SCL driver.

*Test Coverage*:

* STATUS.ACQFULL reached by a 70-byte write the firmware does not service, the ACQ FIFO reset, recovery of the abandoned write, and a 4-byte verify write received and compared. The FIFO levels and the compare are the firmware's.
* The stretch itself, a duration on SCL that no register reports. `tb_i2c0_scl_dut_low` is I2C_0's open-drain pull alone; a target never drives SCL except to stretch, and the FSM enters StretchAcqFull at the ACK phase of the byte in flight when STATUS.ACQFULL asserts, so the window may open on that byte's last clocks. What is required is a pull asserted for a contiguous span far longer than any clock-low phase, still asserted with SCL low over the whole final quarter of the window: the target, not the controller, is holding the clock.
* The release. After the ACQ reset the same pull must be absent throughout the window. The controller may still be clocking the line while it finishes recovering the abandoned write, so the resolved SCL is reported, not required idle.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `i2c_acq_fifo_stretch_reset` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `i2c_acq_fifo_stretch_reset.ecc.hex`, select the shared pad bus, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, arm the wire monitor and a SCRATCH_1 watcher.
. Program the reset vector and release. The image parks at SCRATCH_1 = 0x31 with the ACQ FIFO full and waits for SCRATCH_4 = 0xC10A.
. At 0x31, sample `tb_i2c0_scl_dut_low` across a window of about 20 SCL periods; require it asserted continuously for at least 20 us and through the final quarter of the window with SCL low; read the ACQ level the image published in SCRATCH_5 and require it non-zero. Only then write the acknowledge.
. The image resets the ACQ FIFO, recovers the write and parks at SCRATCH_1 = 0x32. Sample the same window and require the pull absent throughout; read the level from SCRATCH_6 and require zero. Acknowledge.
. Poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic and grade the wire: at least one acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 4 data bytes.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-I2C-ACQ-STRETCH-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-I2C-ACQ-STRETCH-SCL` |At marker 0x31 the target's SCL pull is asserted continuously for at least 20 us and through the final quarter of the window with SCL low, and the ACQ level the firmware published is non-zero
|`CHK-FW-I2C-ACQ-RELEASE-SCL` |At marker 0x32 the target's pull is absent throughout the window (resolved SCL reported, not required idle), and the published ACQ level is zero
|`CHK-FW-I2C-WIRE-TRAFFIC` |At least one acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 4 data bytes: the verify write after recovery crossed the pads
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all four checkers hold. Each acknowledge is written only after its window is graded, so the firmware cannot run past either. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-172-smc_fw_i2c_p1_fifo_depth_xfer_test]]
===== 172 `smc_fw_i2c_p1_fifo_depth_xfer_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_i2c_p1_fifo_depth_xfer_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/i2c_p1_fifo_depth_xfer`, executed by the CPU cluster, I2C_0 as controller against I2C_1 as target on the shared pad bus; `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor` decoding `tb_i2c0_scl/sda`; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: A full-FIFO-depth 64-byte transfer with the controller pushing while the target drains; length and every byte compared, NACKed entries excluded from the payload.

*Test Coverage*:

* A 64-byte payload -- the FIFO depth -- moved one byte per controller write from I2C_0 to I2C_1 while the target drains its ACQ FIFO concurrently; the length and every byte compared, entries classified by ACQ signal so a NACKed byte cannot pass as payload. Those compares are the firmware's.
* The wire, graded by the bench as on card 169: acknowledged write frames to 0x10 carrying at least 64 data bytes in total. Floors, not exact counts, since the driver may split the transfer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `i2c_p1_fifo_depth_xfer` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `i2c_p1_fifo_depth_xfer.ecc.hex`, select the shared pad bus, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, and arm the wire monitor.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Sum the acknowledged data bytes of the write frames to 0x10 and require at least 64.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-I2C-FIFO-DEPTH-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-I2C-WIRE-TRAFFIC` |Acknowledged write frames to 0x10 carrying at least 64 data bytes in total, decoded from the pads
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-173-smc_fw_i2c_tx_stretch_timeout_recovery_test]]
===== 173 `smc_fw_i2c_tx_stretch_timeout_recovery_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_i2c_tx_stretch_timeout_recovery_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/i2c_tx_stretch_timeout_recovery`, executed by the CPU cluster, I2C_1 as controller against I2C_0 as target on the shared pad bus; `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor` decoding `tb_i2c0_scl/sda`; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: The controller's stretch timeout fires on a read the empty target TX FIFO stretches; the recovery -- target disable releases SCL, controller disable drives the automatic STOP, both re-initialised -- ends in a clean 16-byte write.

*Test Coverage*:

* I2C_1 reads from I2C_0 while the target TX FIFO is empty, so the target stretches SCL until the controller stretch timeout fires; the firmware then disables the target to release SCL, disables the controller to exercise the automatic STOP recovery, re-initialises both and checks a 16-byte write. Those compares are the firmware's.
* The wire, graded by the bench as on card 169. For a read the bytes counted are those the target clocked out, since the controller NACKs the last one by design.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `i2c_tx_stretch_timeout_recovery` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `i2c_tx_stretch_timeout_recovery.ecc.hex`, select the shared pad bus, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, and arm the wire monitor.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Grade the decoded frames: at least one acknowledged read frame to 0x10 (the stretched read) and at least one acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 16 data bytes (the recovery write).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-I2C-TX-STRETCH-TIMEOUT-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-I2C-WIRE-TRAFFIC` |Emitted once per floor, twice here: at least one acknowledged read frame to 0x10, and at least one acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 16 data bytes. Both transfers crossed the pads, so the recovery followed a real stretched read
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the boot token and both wire floors hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-174-smc_fw_i2c_fifo_full_test]]
===== 174 `smc_fw_i2c_fifo_full_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_i2c_fifo_full_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/i2c_fifo_full`, executed by the CPU cluster, I2C_0 as controller against I2C_1 as target on the shared pad bus; `SmcFwI2cWireMonitor` decoding `tb_i2c0_scl/sda`; verdict read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: Fill and reset of the four FIFOs of an I2C pair: FMT and TX by software stores to the declared depth, RX and ACQ by real 8-byte transfers, with the driver's software-drain repair flag read back so a repaired FIFO fails.

*Test Coverage*:

* FMT and TX filled by software stores to the depth the RTL declares and reset; RX and ACQ filled by a real 8-byte read and a real 8-byte write between I2C_0 and I2C_1, their levels proven non-zero before the reset and zero after, with the driver's software-drain repair flag read back so a FIFO the driver emptied for it fails instead of passing. Those compares are the firmware's.
* The cocotb `smc_i2c_fifo_full_test` covers the CSR-level full/empty flags only; this leaf is the one that fills RX and ACQ by transfers.
* The wire, graded by the bench as on card 169.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `i2c_fifo_full` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `i2c_fifo_full.ecc.hex`, select the shared pad bus, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, and arm the wire monitor.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Grade the decoded frames: a read frame to 0x10 that returned at least 8 bytes and an acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 8 bytes.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-I2C-FIFO-FULL-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-I2C-WIRE-TRAFFIC` |Emitted once per floor, twice here: a read frame to 0x10 returning at least 8 bytes, and an acknowledged write frame to 0x10 carrying at least 8 bytes -- the transfers that filled RX and ACQ crossed the pads
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the boot token and both wire floors hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-175-smc_fw_uart_log_engine_single_entry_test]]
===== 175 `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_single_entry_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_single_entry_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/uart_log_engine_single_entry`, executed by the CPU cluster, UART0 in MCR.LOOP; rising edges counted on `tb_uart_irq_any`; engine CSRs and the SPM source read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: Log-engine golden path: one 16-byte SRAM entry fetched and written to UART0, returned through MCR.LOOP and read back from RBR in order; LOG_CTRL[0] hwclr; INTR_STATUS 0.

*Test Coverage*:

* The engine's data path is CPU-side -- SRAM fetch, UART write, MCR.LOOP return through the UART's own receiver -- which no cocotb body drives. The image writes 0xA0..0xAF into SPM, points wrap 0's LOG_REGION_ADDR/SIZE at it and LOG_WRITE_ADDR at UART0's THR, puts UART0 in MCR.LOOP with the received-data interrupt enabled, triggers LOG_CTRL[0] = 16, reads the 16 bytes back from RBR in order and requires LOG_CTRL[0] to have hwclr'd and INTR_STATUS to be 0. MCR.LOOP keeps the bytes inside the UART -- `tx_o` stays marking and no pad carries them -- so the byte-for-byte compare is the firmware's and this card does not claim it.
* What the bench observes on its own: the UART interrupt line rose while the image ran, so a looped-back byte did land in the RX FIFO; after PASS the SPM source still holds the pattern the image says it transferred, and the engine reads back programmed at that region with CTRL.EN and MCR.LOOP cleared as the image's cleanup left them.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `uart_log_engine_single_entry` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `uart_log_engine_single_entry.ecc.hex`, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back, and start counting rising edges on `tb_uart_irq_any`.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Read wrap 0's LOG_REGION_ADDR, LOG_REGION_SIZE, LOG_WRITE_ADDR and CTRL and UART0's MCR over SEP_IN and compare with the image's final programming.
. Read the 16-byte SPM source, 64 bits at a time, and compare with 0xA0..0xAF.
. Require the interrupt edge count to be at least one.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-SINGLE-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-CSR-STATE` |Wrap 0 reads back CTRL.EN = 0, REGION_ADDR = SPM + 0x40000, REGION_SIZE = 0x100, WRITE_ADDR = UART0 offset 0 (THR/RBR), and UART0 MCR.LOOP cleared -- the state the image's cleanup leaves, read by the bench rather than reported by the image
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-SPM-PATTERN` |The 16 bytes at the fetch source read 0xA0..0xAF over SEP_IN AXI
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-UART-IRQ` |`tb_uart_irq_any`, the OR over the UART wraps' interrupt outputs, rose at least once during the run. UART0 is the only UART the image programs, so the edge is its received-data interrupt
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all four checkers hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-176-smc_fw_uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure_test]]
===== 176 `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure`, executed by the CPU cluster, UART0 in MCR.LOOP; engine CSRs and the SPM source read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: Log-engine effective-length boundaries -- request shorter than the slot, request clamped to the slot, non-word-aligned slot rounded to one beat -- and a log write under UART TX backpressure.

*Test Coverage*:

* Scenarios A, B, D and E on wrap 0: A, an 8-byte request inside a 16-byte slot (one fetch beat); B, a 64-byte request into a 64-byte slot written against `uart_tx_ready` low -- the transmitter reports ready only while its TX FIFO holds no data, so each byte the engine fetches waits for the serialiser -- its 64 bytes read back in order from RBR while the transfer runs and no 65th byte accepted; D, a 24-byte request clamped to its 16-byte slot; E, a 15-byte slot rounded down to one 8-byte beat. A, D and E read back exactly the requested or clamped byte count from RBR and require no extra byte. Those compares are the firmware's. `INTR_STATUS.LOG_WRITE_ERR` is not a claim of this image: the log-write master targets the wrapper's own UART block, which answers every write OKAY.
* What the bench observes on its own: `tb_uart0_log_write_stalled`, published by `tb_top` as a fetched byte waiting at the wrap-0 log engine's read-data FIFO while its UART transmit-ready input is low, is sampled on every `clk_smc_i` edge while the image runs and must be 1 on at least one cycle and not on all of them: the engine's write FSM was held off with a byte waiting. The transmit-ready input alone is not the observable, because it is also low on cycles where the engine has nothing to write. The count covers the whole image; A, D and E queue eight to twenty-four bytes and can stall the same way, so the count is evidence that the log-write FSM honours `uart_tx_ready`, and B's 64-byte transfer is the scenario whose bytes the firmware compares under it. After the PASS word the SPM source holds E's 0xE0..0xEF in its first 16 bytes and B's 0x50..0x7F behind them -- B was the only scenario to write past byte 16 -- and the engine reads back programmed as E left it, with CTRL.EN and MCR.LOOP cleared by the cleanup.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure.ecc.hex`, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, start sampling `tb_uart0_log_write_stalled`, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Read wrap 0's engine registers and UART0's MCR over SEP_IN and compare with scenario E's programming.
. Read the 64-byte SPM source and compare bytes 0..15 with 0xE0..0xEF and bytes 16..63 with 0x50..0x7F.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-BOUNDARY-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-CSR-STATE` |Wrap 0 reads back as scenario E left it: REGION_SIZE 0xF0 at SPM + 0x40000, WRITE_ADDR = UART0 offset 0 (THR/RBR), CTRL.EN and UART0 MCR.LOOP cleared
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-SPM-PATTERN` |Bytes 0..15 of the source read 0xE0..0xEF and bytes 16..63 read 0x50..0x7F over SEP_IN AXI
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-TX-BACKPRESSURE` |`tb_uart0_log_write_stalled` sampled 1 on at least one and not every `clk_smc_i` cycle while the image ran: a fetched byte waited at the engine's read-data FIFO while the UART reported not ready, so the log-write FSM honours `uart_tx_ready`; the firmware's own compare of B's 64 looped-back bytes is what shows the writes held off during the longest transfer were not dropped
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all four checkers hold. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-177-smc_fw_uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer_test]]
===== 177 `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer`, executed by the CPU cluster, UART0 and UART1 in MCR.LOOP; both wraps' engine CSRs and the SPM source read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: CTRL.EN cleared mid-transfer halts the engine, measured as at least one but fewer bytes than the entry reaching the UART and none after it goes idle; a re-trigger delivers exactly its bytes; four entries triggered together all hwclr; the same halt measurement on an abort from WAIT; replica 1 runs an entry.

*Test Coverage*:

* On wrap 0 with UART0 in MCR.LOOP: a 32-byte entry (as deep as the UART TX FIFO, so the writer is still moving bytes) triggered and CTRL.EN cleared one register access later, the loopback drained as bytes land and required to return at least one and fewer than 32 bytes, all from the slot and none once the UART is idle, with INTR_STATUS required to stay 0 while both error interrupts are enabled; a re-enable and a 16-byte re-trigger that must deliver exactly 16 bytes; four entries fired at once and all four LOG_CTRL words waited to hwclr; another 32-byte transfer aborted from the WAIT state with the same halt measurement and recovery; and a 16-byte entry on replica 1 (wrap 1). Every count, completion and status check is the firmware's.
* What the bench observes on its own: the 256-byte SPM source holds the multi-entry fill 0xC0 + (i & 0x3F) that the later scenarios reuse, and both engines read back programmed at that buffer with CTRL.EN and MCR.LOOP cleared on both wraps by the cleanup.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer.ecc.hex`, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Read both wraps' engine registers and both UARTs' MCR over SEP_IN and compare with the image's final programming.
. Read the 256-byte SPM source and compare with 0xC0 + (i & 0x3F).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-DISABLE-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-CSR-STATE` |For wrap 0 and wrap 1: CTRL.EN = 0, REGION_ADDR = SPM + 0x40000, REGION_SIZE 0x100, WRITE_ADDR = the wrap's own UART offset 0 (THR/RBR), and MCR.LOOP cleared on both UARTs
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-SPM-PATTERN` |The 256 bytes of the source read 0xC0 + (i & 0x3F) over SEP_IN AXI
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold, the CSR compare on both wraps. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''

[[sec-178-smc_fw_uart_log_engine_fetch_err_test]]
===== 178 `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_fetch_err_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_fetch_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SMC C image `fw/tests/uart_log_engine_fetch_err`, executed by the CPU cluster, UART0 in MCR.LOOP; both wraps' engine CSRs, the SPM source and the unmapped address read over SEP_IN AXI

*Objective*: A log-engine fetch from an unmapped address latches INTR_STATUS.LOG_FETCH_ERR: INTR_TEST self-test, INTR_ENABLE gating, LOG_FETCH_ERR as an event (with the engine stopped a W1C holds) and a good fetch then running from a cleared status, sustained DECERR with LOG_CTRL not hwclr, WRITE_ERR staying clear, and the fault on replica 1.

*Test Coverage*:

* The unmapped SMC-local address is derived from the generated map -- the first 4 KB boundary past the WDT cluster, checked at compile time to lie before the reset unit. On wrap 0 the image runs the INTR_TEST self-test for both bits, INTR_ENABLE gating of status capture, a fetch from the unmapped region that must latch LOG_FETCH_ERR, proof that LOG_FETCH_ERR is an event rather than a level (with the engine stopped a W1C holds over a settle window) and that a good fetch then completes from a cleared status which stays clear, a sustained DECERR over a 4 KB region whose LOG_CTRL must not hwclr, the same fault with WRITE_ERR unmasked, and the fault on replica 1. Sixteen CHK tokens are counted inside the image and PASS is refused if any is missing.
* What the bench observes on its own: a SEP_IN AXI read of the unmapped address returns an error response, so the fabric really answers the image's fetch region with DECERR -- the cause the firmware's LOG_FETCH_ERR check rests on; after PASS the SPM region used for the retiring good fetches still holds 0xD0..0xDF, and both engines read back pointed at the unmapped region with CTRL.EN cleared.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `uart_log_engine_fetch_err` through `[c_build.default]`, stage `uart_log_engine_fetch_err.ecc.hex`, hold `boot_stall` from t=0, clear CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 and read the clear back.
. Program the reset vector, release, and poll SCRATCH_0 for the PASS magic.
. Read both wraps' engine registers and UART0's MCR over SEP_IN and compare with the image's final programming.
. Read the 16-byte SPM region used for the retiring good fetches and compare with 0xD0..0xDF.
. Declare the unmapped address to the SEP_IN AXI monitor as expected-error, read it (`csr_read_expect_error`) and require an AXI error response.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-FETCH-ERR-BOOT` |The firmware's own PASS word is read back from CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_0 after a cleared-and-verified start; the image counts its 16 CHK tokens before it posts PASS
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-CSR-STATE` |For wrap 0 and wrap 1: CTRL.EN = 0, REGION_ADDR = the unmapped address, REGION_SIZE 0x100, WRITE_ADDR = the wrap's own UART offset 0 (THR/RBR). Wrap 0's MCR.LOOP is cleared by the cleanup; wrap 1's UART is never programmed by the image and its MCR is not checked
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-SPM-PATTERN` |The 16 bytes at the good-fetch source read 0xD0..0xDF over SEP_IN AXI
|`CHK-FW-LOG-ENGINE-FETCH-DECERR` |A SEP_IN read of the unmapped address returns an AXI error response, so the DECERR the image's fetch met is the fabric's answer to that address and not a property of the engine's bus master alone
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all four checkers hold, the CSR compare on both wraps. No `ci` tag: the hosted runners have no RISC-V toolchain. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw`.

'''''


[[sec-193-smc_rdl_field_sweep_test]]
===== 193 `smc_rdl_field_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_rdl_field_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI) on SEP_IN

*Objective*: Every software-owned field of the SCRATCH, DFX_CTRL, SMC_BASE_CONFIG, CPU_CTRL and RESET_UNIT blocks, plus the adopter strap capture registers, driven against its generated RDL contract and restored.

*Test Coverage*:

* Addresses, reset values, field positions and software-access types come from the PeakRDL IP-XACT export (`hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/ipxact/smc.xml`) through `seq_lib/smc_rdl_regmap.py`, which cross-checks every address against the matching `smc_reg.py` symbol before the sequence uses it. The IP-XACT export is the only generated view that carries the RDL access type, the `oneToSet` / read-side-effect modifiers and the `volatile` flag; the C headers and `smc_reg.py` carry addresses and resets alone.
* Each register is read at reset, written half at a time with its software-writable bits set and then with the low pattern, read back after every half, and restored to its RDL reset. Writing one half at a time deasserts the byte lanes over the other half, so every readback also checks that the untouched half kept its value.
* A register array gets a phased pass: all elements take an index-derived signature, all are read back while the signatures are co-resident, and only then are they restored, so an array folded onto one physical register fails instead of returning the value just written through the same address.
* Fields whose readback the contract does not pin -- hardware-driven, `singlepulse`, `oneToSet`, read-side-effect -- are never written by the generic cycle. The four CPU_CTRL registers that carry a contract of their own (`WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET`, `MUTEX`, `SEMA`, `REFERENCE_COUNTER`) are driven against that contract instead.
* `_EXCLUDED` in the sequence names every remaining software-writable register of those blocks and what driving it here would do; `_assert_partition` fails when a register of the blocks is in neither list, so a regenerated map cannot add one silently.
* The adopter strap capture registers are the `sw = r` half: `hw/sys/smc/doc/memmap.adoc` places `STRAPS_LO` / `STRAPS_HI` at BASE + 0x040_5800 / 0x040_5804 inside the adopter external window, and straps.rdl makes both `sw = r; hw = w`. What they captured at cold reset is read once and held against the write, not predicted from the integration; the contract proven is that every undeclared bit reads 0 and a software write changes nothing.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_rdl_field_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-RDL-SWEEP-CYCLE` |Every swept register instance read its RDL reset with all undeclared bits 0, took the pattern its access type allows through half-register writes and the co-resident array pass, and read back its RDL reset after the restore
|`CHK-RDL-SWEEP-SPECIAL` |CPU_CTRL `WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET` self-cleared its `singlepulse` fields, `MUTEX[0..3]` handed the mutex out once and released it on a write, `SEMA[0..3]` added and subtracted the signed written value, and `REFERENCE_COUNTER` dropped below its earlier sample once a software write of 0 had crossed into the reference-clock domain, then advanced again from the loaded value
|`CHK-RDL-STRAPS-RO` |`STRAPS_LO` and `STRAPS_HI` read with every undeclared bit 0, and a software write of 0xffffffff left both unchanged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold and the protocol-VIP record clears its 521-access floor. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-194-smc_cpu_l2_read_wedge_test]]
===== 194 `smc_cpu_l2_read_wedge_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cpu_l2_read_wedge_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI) on SEP_IN; TB holds and drops SEP_IN / SYS_IN R

*Objective*: A timeout-forced CPU reset flushes a pending L2 read isolate while the fabric still withholds R, then a fresh L2 read completes.

*Test Coverage*:

* `RESET_TIMEOUT` force mode applies the CPU reset while L2 pending AR is nonzero and the uncore reset is still withheld.
* The isolate reports drained while SEP_IN R remains held.
* Stale R beats are dropped on SEP_IN and SYS_IN; the pending AR count stays 0 while they are consumed.
* After isolate release a new L2 read returns the recovery word.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cpu_l2_read_wedge_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-FORCED-RESET` |Force-mode timeout applied the CPU and uncore reset
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-DRAINED-WHILE-BLOCKED` |`tb_cpu_drained` rose while the original R hold was still asserted
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-STALE-R-DISCARDED` |Stale R beats drained to 0 and L2 pending AR stayed 0
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-L2-READ-RECOVERED` |Post-reset L2 read returned the expected word
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-CLUSTER-REOPENED` |L2 isolate released and the recovery read completed
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all five checkers hold. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `cpu`.

'''''

[[sec-195-smc_cpu_l2_write_wedge_test]]
===== 195 `smc_cpu_l2_write_wedge_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cpu_l2_write_wedge_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI) on SEP_IN; TB pauses the W channel after AW

*Objective*: A timeout-forced CPU reset flushes a partial L2 write, absorbs late W beats, then a fresh L2 write and readback complete.

*Test Coverage*:

* Reset is requested before the partial-W wedge, so the leaf proves late-W cleanup after a reset is already in flight.
* Force mode applies the reset; pending AW and W counts clear while W is still paused.
* Releasing the W pause leaves the pending W count at 0 (late beats absorbed).
* After isolate release an L2 write and checked readback complete.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cpu_l2_write_wedge_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-FORCED-RESET` |Force-mode timeout applied the CPU and uncore reset
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-DRAINED-WHILE-BLOCKED` |Isolate reports drained while W is still paused
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-LATE-W-ABSORBED` |Late W beats left the pending W count at 0
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-L2-WRITE-RECOVERED` |Post-reset L2 write and readback completed
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-CLUSTER-REOPENED` |L2 isolate released and the recovery write completed
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all five checkers hold. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `cpu`.

'''''

[[sec-196-smc_cpu_mmio_read_wedge_test]]
===== 196 `smc_cpu_mmio_read_wedge_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cpu_mmio_read_wedge_test`

*BFM / Model*: `mmio_wedge` firmware on SYS_OUT; TB holds the SYS_OUT R beat

*Objective*: Firmware issues a SYS_OUT read whose R is held; a timeout-forced reset absorbs the stale response; firmware reboots and the read completes.

*Test Coverage*:

* Requires the `mmio_wedge` image and `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`.
* Force mode applies the reset while MMIO pending AR is nonzero.
* Releasing the held R advances the SYS_OUT response count by one and the MMIO isolate stays held.
* Phase-2 firmware completes exactly one external read at the wedge address; a SEP_IN front-port read then completes.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cpu_mmio_read_wedge_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-FORCED-RESET` |Force-mode timeout applied the CPU and uncore reset
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-DRAINED-WHILE-BLOCKED` |Isolate reports drained while SYS_OUT R is still held
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-STALE-RESP-ABSORBED` |Exactly one stale SYS_OUT R was absorbed; MMIO isolate stayed held
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-FW-RECOVERED` |Phase-2 firmware completed exactly one external read
|`CHK-CPU-ISO-FLUSH-FRONT-PORT-RECOVERED` |SEP_IN front-port read completed after isolate release
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all five checkers hold. Enrolled in: `all`, `cpu`. Not in `hosted` (builds firmware).

'''''

[[sec-198-smc_gpio_intf_regblock_sweep_test]]
===== 198 `smc_gpio_intf_regblock_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_gpio_intf_regblock_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Cycle GPIO_INTF DATA_CTRL_ENABLE and ACCESS_FILTER on all 65 instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* DATA_CTRL_ENABLE takes the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of its three override fields through half-register writes, so the byte lanes over the other half are deasserted on every write.
* ACCESS_FILTER drives both filter enables and both AxPROT requirement fields to all-ones and all-zeros in an order that keeps the register reachable: the read requirement first while nothing is gated, then the enables with the write requirement at 0, then the read requirement cleared, then the write requirement.
* The armed state carries an AxPROT allow leg and an AxPROT deny leg on every instance.
* Both registers are restored to their generated RDL reset word.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_gpio_intf_regblock_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-GPIO-INTF-DATA-CTRL-ENABLE-SWEEP` |All 65 DATA_CTRL_ENABLE instances read their RDL reset, carried the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of their software-writable fields after each half-register write, drove no bit outside the fields the RDL declares, and returned to that reset
|`CHK-GPIO-INTF-ACCESS-FILTER-SWEEP` |All 65 ACCESS_FILTER instances took both filter enables and both AxPROT requirement fields to all-ones and all-zeros, matched the predicted word after every step, and returned to the RDL reset 0x0001_0100
|`CHK-GPIO-INTF-ACCESS-FILTER-DENY` |On each of the 65 instances the armed read filter returned the programmed word to the matching-AxPROT read and refused the non-matching read of the same address with DECERR and the error-slave word, so the refusal is the AxPROT gate and not a dead window
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-GPIO-INTF-ACCESS-FILTER-DENY`, `CHK-GPIO-INTF-ACCESS-FILTER-SWEEP`, `CHK-GPIO-INTF-DATA-CTRL-ENABLE-SWEEP` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-199-smc_filter_config_field_sweep_test]]
===== 199 `smc_filter_config_field_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_filter_config_field_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Cycle every FILTER_CONFIG field and the write-once lock on all 32 filter entries.

*Test Coverage*:

* Each of the 16 inbound and 16 outbound entries takes the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of every software-writable FILTER_CONFIG field through half-register writes and is restored to its RDL reset.
* The ones pattern sets `entry_enabled` in the same write as `read_allowed` and `write_allowed`, and leaves `START_ADDR`/`END_ADDR` at their reset, so an armed entry admits rather than blocks.
* The write-once `locked` field is set on every entry after all field cycles have completed, and the locked entry then refuses both a write of 0 over that bit and a write of the other half.
* The sequence leaves all 32 entries locked; only a reset clears the field.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_filter_config_field_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FILTER-CONFIG-FIELD-SWEEP` |All 32 entries read their RDL reset, carried the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of their software-writable fields after each half-register write, drove no bit outside the fields the RDL declares, held the reset of the bits software cannot write, and returned to that reset
|`CHK-FILTER-CONFIG-LOCK-WOSET` |On all 32 entries the write-once `locked` field went from clear to set on a write of 1 and the entry then refused both later configuration writes with an error response while still reading the locked word, so the refused writes took no effect; the accepted writes of the field cycle on the same addresses are the live control
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-FILTER-CONFIG-FIELD-SWEEP`, `CHK-FILTER-CONFIG-LOCK-WOSET` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-200-smc_i2c_intr_reg_sweep_test]]
===== 200 `smc_i2c_intr_reg_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_intr_reg_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Cycle the I2C interrupt and event registers on all three instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* INTR_ENABLE and INTR_TEST take the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of the fields the generated contract pins, through half-register writes, and are restored to their reset.
* A write of the write-only INTR_TEST fields raises the matching INTR_STATE events and a write of the same mask back into INTR_STATE clears them.
* CONTROLLER_EVENTS and TARGET_EVENTS take a full-width and a half-register write of their whole declared mask.
* TARGET_ACK_CTRL and ACQ_FIFO_NEXT_DATA, the two read-mux rows no other leaf reads, are read under a STATUS word showing the block idle with both FIFOs empty.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_intr_reg_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-INTR-REG-SWEEP` |On all three instances INTR_ENABLE and INTR_TEST read their RDL reset, carried the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of their software-writable fields after each half-register write, drove no bit outside the fields the RDL declares, and returned to that reset
|`CHK-I2C-INTR-TEST-STATE` |On all three instances a write of the write-only INTR_TEST fields raised exactly those INTR_STATE events from a measured clear state, and a write of the same mask back into INTR_STATE cleared them again
|`CHK-I2C-EVENTS-W1C` |All six CONTROLLER_EVENTS and TARGET_EVENTS instances read clear, took a full-width and a half-register write of their whole declared mask, and read clear after both, which is the `oneToClear` contract for a field no bus event has set
|`CHK-I2C-READMUX-TAIL` |All six TARGET_ACK_CTRL and ACQ_FIFO_NEXT_DATA reads returned their RDL reset word with no bit outside the declared fields, under a STATUS word matching its RDL reset
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-I2C-EVENTS-W1C`, `CHK-I2C-INTR-REG-SWEEP`, `CHK-I2C-INTR-TEST-STATE`, `CHK-I2C-READMUX-TAIL` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-201-smc_uart_log_engine_field_sweep_test]]
===== 201 `smc_uart_log_engine_field_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_uart_log_engine_field_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Cycle the UART, divisor-latch and log-engine registers on all four wrapper instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* IER, ITR, SCR, ECR, MCR and LCR take the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of their fields through half-register writes and are restored to their reset. LCR runs last, so the DLAB its ones pattern sets is back at 0 before the divisor-latch leg.
* DLL and DLM are written and read behind `LCR.DLAB`, held across a clear and a re-raise of DLAB, and restored; with DLAB clear the address DLM occupies returns IER's own reset word.
* The log engine's INTR_ENABLE takes the same cycle, and a write of the write-only INTR_TEST fields raises and then clears the matching INTR_STATUS events.
* All 16 LOG_CTRL elements of each wrapper take an index-unique log length and are read back while the 16 signatures are co-resident. The signatures stay resident: a nonzero LOG_LEN is a pending arbiter request that must hold until granted, so only the hardware clears it. `log_engine/CTRL.EN` stays at its reset 0, so no log transfer starts.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_uart_log_engine_field_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-UART-16550-FIELD-SWEEP` |On all four instances the six writable UART registers read their RDL reset, carried the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of their fields after each half-register write, drove no bit outside the fields the RDL declares, and returned to that reset
|`CHK-UART-16550-DL-WINDOW` |On all four instances both divisor latches took the all-ones and the reset value behind LCR.DLAB and still held the written value after DLAB was cleared and raised again, and the address DLM occupies returned IER's own reset word while DLAB was clear, so the window really switched
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-SWEEP` |On all four log engines INTR_ENABLE took the all-ones and all-zeros pattern through half-register writes, and a write of the write-only INTR_TEST fields raised exactly those INTR_STATUS events from a measured clear state and a write of the same mask cleared them again
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-LOG-CTRL-SWEEP` |All 64 LOG_CTRL elements returned their own index-unique log length while all 16 elements of their wrapper held theirs, so an array folded onto one physical register fails
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-INTR-SWEEP`, `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-LOG-CTRL-SWEEP`, `CHK-UART-16550-DL-WINDOW`, `CHK-UART-16550-FIELD-SWEEP` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-202-smc_log_engine_transfer_test]]
===== 202 `smc_log_engine_transfer_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_log_engine_transfer_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the wrapper's own UART in `MCR.LOOP`

*Objective*: Run a real log-engine transfer on all four uart_log_engine_wrap instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* The software flow of `hw/ip/uart/log_engine/doc/programming.adoc`, driven over SEP_IN AXI rather than from firmware: four logs are written into the SPM log region of each wrapper, LOG_REGION_ADDR and LOG_REGION_SIZE are pointed at them, LOG_WRITE_ADDR at the wrapper's own transmit holding register, the UART is put in `MCR.LOOP`, the engine is enabled, and all four LOG_CTRL elements are triggered at once so the round-robin arbiter has to grant more than one requester.
* Each log fills its whole slot, which `log_engine.rdl` sizes as the region size over the sixteen LOG_CTRL elements rounded down to complete 8-byte fetch beats, so every element's fetch has to come back for a second beat before it is done.
* Every byte names the wrapper, the element and the slot offset it came from, so a stream that carried a neighbour's slot, repeated a byte or dropped one fails.
* The order the arbiter interleaves the four elements in is not predicted; each element's own bytes are required to arrive in slot order, exactly once.
* Nothing writes a pending LOG_CTRL length back to 0. The only clearing this leaf takes is the hardware one it waits for.
* The UART divisor latches, line and modem control, FIFO control and the wrapper pad-mux enable are all restored, and the engine is disabled with its region and write addresses cleared.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_log_engine_transfer_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-TRANSFER-BYTES` |All 128 log bytes of the run reached the receiver of their own wrapper, every element's bytes arrived in slot order and exactly once, and no byte named another element or another wrapper; a transfer that never started fails on the byte count before the compare is reached
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-TRANSFER-HWCLR` |Every driven LOG_CTRL element read a length of 0 after its log arrived, and nothing in the leaf wrote a pending length back, so the clear is the hardware completion the RDL declares
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-TRANSFER-CLEAN` |Every LSR read during the transfer showed no overrun, parity, framing or break error, the receiver went quiet after the last expected byte, and INTR_STATUS read clear before and after, so the engine hit neither a fetch nor a write error and sent nothing it was not asked to
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-TRANSFER-BYTES`, `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-TRANSFER-CLEAN`, `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-TRANSFER-HWCLR` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-203-smc_zeroer_multi_burst_test]]
===== 203 `smc_zeroer_multi_burst_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_zeroer_multi_burst_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the TB SYS_OUT output-fabric responder

*Objective*: Clear a region larger than one AXI burst and check both halves.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_zeroer_sanity_test` clears eight bytes and its busy probe clears 0x800, both of which fit one AXI4 burst -- `AxLEN` is eight bits, so a burst carries at most 256 transfers, which on the 64-bit output path is 0x800 bytes. This leaf programs 0x1000, which no single burst can carry, so the zeroer has to return from its data phase to a second address phase.
* Four probe words -- the first and last word of each half of the region -- are poisoned with distinct values beforehand and read back to prove the poison landed, then required to read zero.
* A witness word immediately past the end of the region keeps its own poison, so a job that ran past its size fails.
* The output responder's write-transaction counter has to advance by at least the two bursts the size demands, and `CTRL_STATUS.STATUS` has to leave the level it rests at while the zeroer is idle and return to it. Which level means busy is not asserted, as in `smc_zeroer_sanity_test`.
* `SIZE` and `DEST_ADDR` are cleared afterwards, so a later write to `CTRL_STATUS` -- the register whose write side effect is the trigger -- cannot start a job over anything.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_zeroer_multi_burst_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ZEROER-MULTI-BURST-PRELOAD` |The four probe words spanning both halves of the job and the witness word past its end were poisoned with distinct values and read back exactly, so the zero readbacks that follow can fail
|`CHK-ZEROER-MULTI-BURST-STATUS` |`CTRL_STATUS.STATUS` left the level it rests at while the zeroer is idle and returned to it, and the output responder booked at least the two write transactions a 0x1000-byte job needs under AxLEN's 256-transfer cap, so the zeroer took a second address phase after its first data phase
|`CHK-ZEROER-MULTI-BURST-ZEROED` |All four probe words read 0 after the job, including the first and last word of each half, and the witness word immediately past the end still holds its poison
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-ZEROER-MULTI-BURST-PRELOAD`, `CHK-ZEROER-MULTI-BURST-STATUS`, `CHK-ZEROER-MULTI-BURST-ZEROED` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-204-smc_cool_reset_zero_delay_test]]
===== 204 `smc_cool_reset_zero_delay_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cool_reset_zero_delay_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); `cfg_flr_pf_active_i` driven as a TB input

*Objective*: Run an FLR cool reset with no pre-reset delay programmed.

*Test Coverage*:

* `reset_unit.rdl` makes `ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_COUNTER_VALUE` the wait before the cool asserts and `ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_RESET_COUNTER_VALUE` the hold, and records that a hold of 0 prevents initiation whatever the pre-delay is. So the hold decides whether a cool happens and the pre-delay only decides when. `smc_cool_reset_from_pcie_test` always programs a non-zero pre-delay.
* Deny leg: with both counters at their reset 0 the FLR request has to latch in `ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG` and no cool may follow.
* Allow leg: with the same pre-delay of 0 and a non-zero hold the cool has to assert inside the synchroniser allowance -- a reset unit that counted a programmed delay anyway runs past the bound -- and then release.
* A warm-domain scratch register written before the cool reads 0 after it, which is the DUT-side evidence that a cool went through rather than a pin wiggle.
* The isolate-request pad is held low throughout so the FLR path is the only isolation source, `cfg_flr_pf_active_i` is dropped before the recovery wait, the warm reset is waited for before any further CSR access, and the latched request and the hold counter are cleared at the end.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cool_reset_zero_delay_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FLR-ZERO-DELAY-DENY` |The FLR request latched in `ISOLATE_REQ_SMC_REG` with both counters at their reset 0 and no cool followed within the watched window, which is the RDL's "a value of 0 will prevent initiation" for the hold counter
|`CHK-FLR-ZERO-DELAY-COOL` |With the pre-delay at 0 and a non-zero hold the cool asserted inside the synchroniser allowance, so no programmed pre-delay was counted, was held and released, and the warm-domain scratch register that carried a pattern beforehand reads 0 afterwards
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-FLR-ZERO-DELAY-COOL`, `CHK-FLR-ZERO-DELAY-DENY` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-205-smc_log_engine_abort_test]]
===== 205 `smc_log_engine_abort_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_log_engine_abort_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the wrapper's own UART in `MCR.LOOP`

*Objective*: Halt a log-engine transfer with CTRL.EN on all four wrapper instances and bring the engine back.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_log_engine_transfer_test` runs transfers to completion. `log_engine.rdl` gives `CTRL.EN` the other half of its contract -- "When unset, disables the Log Engine and resets all FSMs, flops, and FIFOs" -- and no leaf has asked it to stop a transfer in flight.
* Each wrapper is given one log filling its whole slot, which this region makes twice the UART transmit FIFO the `uart_log_engine_disable_during_xfer` firmware image pins and several fetch beats deep, so neither the fetch nor the write side can be finished when the disable lands one register access after the first byte is seen.
* Three legs, an allow followed by a deny followed by an allow, so the deny cannot pass on a dead bus: the transfer starts and bytes reach the receiver; `CTRL.EN` is cleared and, once the UART reports itself idle, fewer than the whole log has arrived, what did arrive is the head of that slot in order, and a second drain finds nothing more; a shorter length written into the same element while the engine is still disabled is delivered in full once `CTRL.EN` is set again.
* Draining until the UART reports transmitter-empty with no received data, for a run of polls longer than a character time, is how the firmware image measures the same thing, and it is what lets the halt be checked without knowing how many bytes the UART had buffered.
* Nothing writes a pending LOG_CTRL length back to 0: the aborted length is replaced by a shorter non-zero one, so the arbiter request the element holds never drops before the hardware clear that ends it.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_log_engine_abort_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-ABORT-HALTED` |On every wrapper a transfer was in flight -- bytes had reached the receiver -- when `CTRL.EN` was cleared, and once the UART went idle at least one and fewer than the whole log had arrived, each byte the head of that wrapper's own slot in order, with a second drain finding nothing more
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-ABORT-LENGTH-KEPT` |LOG_CTRL still read the length it was given while the engine was disabled, which is the RDL's reset of the FSMs, flops and FIFOs with the registers preserved
|`CHK-LOG-ENGINE-ABORT-RETRIGGER` |A shorter length written into the same element while the engine was disabled delivered exactly its bytes from the head of the slot once `CTRL.EN` was set again, hardware cleared the length, and INTR_STATUS stayed clear across both the halt and the re-trigger
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-ABORT-HALTED`, `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-ABORT-LENGTH-KEPT`, `CHK-LOG-ENGINE-ABORT-RETRIGGER` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-206-smc_periph_regblock_sweep_test]]
===== 206 `smc_periph_regblock_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_periph_regblock_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Cycle the peripheral register blocks no other leaf writes against their RDL contract.

*Test Coverage*:

* The AVSBus controller, the three telemetry receivers, the OCTS system timer, the eFuse interface controller, the DMA controller configuration and the four log engines' region and enable registers have never had a write from this package. Each takes the cycle the other register sweeps use: read at reset, the all-ones and the all-zeros pattern of the fields the generated contract pins, each half written on its own so the byte lanes over the other half are deasserted, then the RDL reset restored.
