* Only fields the contract makes plain read-write are driven, so a `singlepulse` trigger, a `oneToClear` event and a hardware-driven status are never written by the generic cycle.
* `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` additionally holds `efuse_data` and `program_enable` at their reset, on top of the `singlepulse` `efuse_program_go` the contract already excludes, so no combination the sweep writes can arm a fuse burn. Its address and read-back select are driven normally.
* `AVS_CFG_1` carries the AVS clock mux and divider, and `avsbus_controller.rdl` says to gate the clocks entering the mux before changing either and to turn them back on afterwards, which is the order this cycle takes. The gate is on the AVS protocol clock, not the register interface.
* The OCTS system timer's `CTRL` does not take the all-ones and all-zeros patterns. `system_timer_octs.rdl` makes `CREDIT_VAL` "must be greater than PULSE_WIDTH" and `PULSE_WIDTH` "must be less than CREDIT_VAL" a software constraint, and `system_timer_octs_core.sv` holds a run-time assertion to it; all ones makes the two equal and all zeros makes `CREDIT_VAL` zero. The register takes two constrained cycles instead, whose four patterns between them drive every bit of all three fields both ways while every word written keeps `CREDIT_VAL` above the effective `PULSE_WIDTH` -- effective because the RDL rounds a `PULSE_WIDTH` of 0 up to 1. The sequence checks that relation host-side for every word, including the RDL reset it restores, before issuing any access, and both constrained fields sit in the same half of the word so the half-register writes never leave an illegal pair resident.
* The telemetry `INTR_TEST.BUFFER_THRESHOLD` field is plain storage, so driving it raises an event; each receiver's INTR_STATUS is cleared afterwards by a write of its declared mask and re-read, which is that register's `oneToClear` contract.
* The DMA controller's block answers a sub-word write with an error response, so its configuration takes the same cycle at full width instead of in halves.
* `log_engine/CTRL` is swept last, so the enable is only ever set while the region and write addresses are back at their reset and no LOG_CTRL element holds a length, and no transfer starts.
* `AVS_CMD`, `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR`, the telemetry counters, the DMA transfer descriptors and the eFuse read and program data registers are not driven: each is a command, an event clear or a hardware-owned value with a leaf of its own or no written-value expectation.

* `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS` is the one register of the eFuse block no leaf reaches. Four of its fields are `sw = r; hw = w` and its three clears are write-only `singlepulse`, so the half-register cycle has nothing to drive and a sweep of it would assert rather than run. What it can be held to is checked instead: a read reports an idle block with no error bit and no clear bit set, a write of all three clears is accepted, and the register then reads back exactly what it read before -- so no clear stayed set and no `sw = r` state field moved under the write. The masks come from the generated `efuse_interface_ctrl.h`.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_periph_regblock_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PERIPH-REGBLOCK-SINGLE-SWEEP` |Every single-instance peripheral register read its RDL reset, carried the all-ones and all-zeros pattern of its driven fields after each write, drove no bit outside the fields the RDL declares, held the reset of the bits software cannot write, and returned to that reset
|`CHK-PERIPH-TELEMETRY-SWEEP` |All three telemetry receivers cycled INTR_ENABLE, INTR_TEST and CTRL against their contract and were restored, and the events the test field raised were then cleared out of INTR_STATUS by a write of its declared mask
|`CHK-PERIPH-LOG-ENGINE-SWEEP` |All four log engines cycled their region size, region address, write address and enable against their contract and were restored, with the enable set only while the addresses were at their reset
|`CHK-PERIPH-REGBLOCK-COMPARES` |The scoreboard booked at least the expected number of exact-value compares of its own for the reads whose whole word the RDL contract predicts, independently of the sequence's counters
|`CHK-PERIPH-EFUSE-STATUS` |EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS read an idle block with no error bit and no clear bit set, took a write of all three of its write-only singlepulse clears, and read back exactly what it read before
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-PERIPH-EFUSE-STATUS`, `CHK-PERIPH-LOG-ENGINE-SWEEP`, `CHK-PERIPH-REGBLOCK-COMPARES`, `CHK-PERIPH-REGBLOCK-SINGLE-SWEEP`, `CHK-PERIPH-TELEMETRY-SWEEP` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-207-smc_uart_char_loopback_test]]
===== 207 `smc_uart_char_loopback_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_uart_char_loopback_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); each wrapper's own UART in `MCR.LOOP`

*Objective*: Send characters through every UART's own loopback and check the registers they move.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_uart_log_engine_field_sweep_test` writes the UART's software-owned registers and the log-engine leaves read LSR and RBR while the engine feeds the transmitter, but nothing drives the UART's own transmit holding register and nothing writes the addresses the read-only registers occupy. The write side of RBR, IIR, LSR and MSR and the external-register handshake behind RBR have therefore never been exercised.
* FCR is write-only, so its enable is checked where its effect shows: `uart_16550_main.rdl` gives `IIR.FIFOS_ENABLED` the value `0x3` with the FIFOs on and `0x0` with them off, and the leaf drives FCR both ways and requires IIR to follow. `FCR.RCVR_FIFO_RESET` is `singlepulse`, and its effect is `LSR.DR` going away with a character still unread.
* Six distinct bytes are written into THR and compared against what comes back out of RBR on each wrapper, with the transmitter required empty before the first and the receiver quiet after the last. That is also the only access that reaches the external-register interface RBR sits behind.
* With `IER.ERBFI` set and a character waiting, IIR has to report an interrupt pending -- `INTERRUPT_PENDING` is active low -- with the Received Data Ready id `0x2` the RDL names, and no interrupt pending once RBR is drained.
* `hw/ip/uart/uart_16550/doc/programming.adoc` fixes the loopback modem mapping: with `MCR.LOOP` set, CTS follows RTS, DSR follows DTR, RI follows OUT1 and DCD follows OUT2. Raising the four outputs must raise exactly those four MSR levels and dropping them must clear them. The sticky deltas separate on the same two legs: DCTS, DDSR and DDCD set on either edge, while TERI sets only on the trailing edge of RI, so the rising leg must leave TERI clear and the falling leg must set it.
* LSR and MSR have no write side, so a write at either address must leave them reading what they read before.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_uart_char_loopback_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-UART-CHAR-LOOPBACK` |Every byte written into the transmit holding register of every UART came back out of the receiver of the same UART unchanged, with the transmitter reported empty before the first and the receiver quiet after the last, and no overrun, parity, framing or break error on any LSR read
|`CHK-UART-FCR-EFFECT` |The write-only FCR was driven both ways and IIR reported the matching `FIFOS_ENABLED` encoding each time, and a write of the singlepulse receive-FIFO reset with a character waiting made LSR stop reporting received data
|`CHK-UART-IIR-RX-READY` |IIR reported an interrupt pending with the Received Data Ready id while a character waited and the received-data interrupt was enabled, and none pending once RBR was drained
|`CHK-UART-MSR-LOOPBACK` |Raising all four MCR outputs raised exactly the four MSR levels the programming guide maps them to with DCTS, DDSR and DDCD set and TERI clear; dropping them cleared the levels and set all four deltas including TERI; and a write at the LSR and MSR addresses left both reading what they read before
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-UART-CHAR-LOOPBACK`, `CHK-UART-FCR-EFFECT`, `CHK-UART-IIR-RX-READY`, `CHK-UART-MSR-LOOPBACK` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-208-smc_efuse_lock_guard_test]]
===== 208 `smc_efuse_lock_guard_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_efuse_lock_guard_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the default eFuse image in the adopter bank model

*Objective*: Establish a software lock on three eFuse shadow fields and check the guard's answer.

*Test Coverage*:

* `hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc` gives every shadow field a 4-bit `lock` permission reachable two ways, and says the guard (`efuse_guard.sv`) enforces whichever of the hardware and software locks is more restrictive. The software lock is the `LOCKS` CSR, "write-1-to-set", so firmware can tighten access but never loosen it without a reset.
* `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test` drives that path on `JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY` but needs its own preload asset and is held out of the regression, so nothing in the scheduled run establishes a software lock at all.
* Three fields -- the first two SPARE entries and the OCCP transport timeout, whose locks the default image leaves clear -- each take four steps, the first being the live control for the two denies: a pattern written and read back while unlocked; the write lock set and a different pattern shown not to land; `LOCKS` written again with that bit clear and the bit shown still set; the read lock set and the read shown not to disclose the field.
* The sequence reads `LOCKS` first and fails if any bit it is about to set is already set by the image, so it never mistakes a pre-set lock for one it established.
* The response to a write-locked write is recorded rather than asserted: neither the RDL nor the architecture document fixes whether the guard refuses or ignores it, and the claim is that the write did not land. Likewise the read-locked read claims non-disclosure only, because the document states the permission but not the substituted word.
* `SMC_CONFIG` and `JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY` are left alone: the first carries boot configuration other logic reads, the second is the held-out leaf's subject. A software lock is cleared only by reset, so the leaf leaves its six bits set and is terminal for those three fields.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_efuse_lock_guard_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-PRE` |LOCKS read the eFuse image word with every lock bit the sequence goes on to set still clear, so each deny that follows is against a lock the sequence established itself
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-WRITE` |On all three fields a pattern written while unlocked read back exactly, and a different pattern written with the write lock set did not land -- the field still read the first pattern -- and the guard answered the refused write instead of wedging the bus
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-WOSET` |On all three fields a later write of LOCKS with the field's write-lock bit clear left it set, which is the `onwrite = woset` contract the RDL gives every lock
|`CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-READ` |On all three fields a read taken with the read lock set returned something other than the word the field holds, so the guard withheld the content
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-PRE`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-READ`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-WOSET`, `CHK-EFUSE-LOCK-GUARD-WRITE` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-209-smc_cpu_ctrl_read_only_write_test]]
===== 209 `smc_cpu_ctrl_read_only_write_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_cpu_ctrl_read_only_write_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Write at every CPU_CTRL address the RDL makes read-only, and write 0 into the singlepulse reset fields.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_rdl_field_sweep_test` owns CPU_CTRL and drives every register the RDL makes writable, but never writes at the addresses of the registers it makes `sw = r`, so the decode row those addresses take on a write has never been exercised.
* `TEST_CTRL`, `SMC_ATTRIBUTES` and the head of each of the four per-core writeback program-counter arrays are written with all ones and required to read the same word afterwards as before. The sequence asserts each one's `rw_mask` is zero first, so a register that gained a writable field cannot be written at here by accident.
* `WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET` takes a full-width write of 0, which `smc_rdl_field_sweep_test` never does -- it writes the fields set and then writes 0 only over the half they do not occupy. Its four `reset_cycle_count` fields are `singlepulse`, so nothing is stored either way and the register has to read 0 afterwards. `CORE_RESET_PULSE_COUNT` is read either side as a second, weaker observation -- it carries the configured pulse durations and a bit that reads low while a core reset is in progress -- and does not catch a pulse that began and ended between the two reads.
* `RESET_CTRL` is not written. It carries the per-core and uncore reset levels and the four core reset-pulse starts, which is why `smc_rdl_field_sweep_test` excludes it, and a write of ones into it would reset the running bench.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_cpu_ctrl_read_only_write_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-CPU-CTRL-READ-ONLY-WRITE` |All six CPU_CTRL registers every field of which the RDL makes `sw = r` read the same word after a write of all ones at their address as before it
|`CHK-CPU-CTRL-WDT-ZERO-WRITE` |A full-width write of 0 into the four singlepulse reset fields of WDT_TIMEOUT_RESET left the register reading 0, so nothing it carried was stored, and CORE_RESET_PULSE_COUNT read the same word either side with no core reset in progress when the second read landed
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-CPU-CTRL-READ-ONLY-WRITE`, `CHK-CPU-CTRL-WDT-ZERO-WRITE` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-210-smc_dma_reg_byte_enable_error_test]]
===== 210 `smc_dma_reg_byte_enable_error_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_dma_reg_byte_enable_error_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Refuse a sub-word write at every writable DMA register, and show the refusal takes no effect.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_periph_regblock_sweep_test` found that this block answers a sub-word write with an error, which is why its configuration takes the full-width cycle there. The generated block says why: `reg_error = (devmode_i & addrmiss) | wr_err` and `wr_err = reg_we & (addr_hit[N] & |(PERMIT[N] & ~reg_be))`, so a write errors when it omits a byte lane that register's `PERMIT` mask requires -- not merely when it is narrow. Every register update is gated on `!reg_error`.
* That distinction decides where the write lands. `PERMIT` covers the lanes a register's fields occupy and all thirteen writable registers have fields in their low half, so a two-byte write at the register's own address can leave `PERMIT` satisfied and be accepted. The write therefore goes to the upper half, which omits the low lanes of all thirteen.
* Each of the thirteen -- the configuration word and the twelve transfer-descriptor registers -- takes four accesses: a full-width read that records what it holds; a full-width write of that same value, which has to be accepted and is the live control that the address is writable at all; the two-byte write over the upper half, which has to be refused; and a full-width read that has to return the recorded value.
* The last step is also why the leaf is safe against the transfer descriptors: a refused write never reaches the register. The second writes back the value already there. No transfer is started -- in this frontend the launch is a read of a `NEXT_ID` register, which is read-only and outside the set this leaf touches.
* The read-only registers are left alone. Their uncovered rows pair `addr_hit` with `reg_re` and a set `reg_error`, which no stimulus can produce: `reg_re` and `reg_we` are mutually exclusive, `wr_err` is gated on `reg_we`, and `addrmiss` needs no address to hit.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_dma_reg_byte_enable_error_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DMA-REG-BYTE-ENABLE-ERROR` |At each of the thirteen writable DMA registers a full-width write of the word it already held was accepted and a two-byte write over its upper half was refused with an error response, so the refusal is the missing byte lanes and not a dead window
|`CHK-DMA-REG-REFUSED-WRITE-NO-EFFECT` |Every one of the thirteen read back the word it held before its refused write, so the byte-enable error gates the register update rather than being reported after it
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-DMA-REG-BYTE-ENABLE-ERROR`, `CHK-DMA-REG-REFUSED-WRITE-NO-EFFECT` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-211-smc_uart_core_mode_select_test]]
===== 211 `smc_uart_core_mode_select_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_uart_core_mode_select_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); each wrapper's own UART in `MCR.LOOP`, and wrapper 0 in `MCR.LINE_LOOPBACK`

*Objective*: Drive the three UART mode selectors no other leaf moves, and check what each one is specified to do.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every UART leaf in the package configures the UART the same way -- the FIFOs enabled, DMA mode 0, the receive trigger at one character and `MCR.LOOP` for the loopback -- so three of the core's mode selectors have never left their reset or their setup value on any wrapper: `FCR.FIFO_ENABLE` cleared, `FCR.DMA_MODE_SELECT` set, and `MCR.LINE_LOOPBACK` set.
* `hw/ip/uart/uart_16550/doc/architecture.adoc` describes the two receive paths the 16550 has -- software reads "from RBR or FIFO" -- and `FCR.FIFO_ENABLE` resets clear. A character is left unread in the receive FIFO and the FIFOs are then turned off: `IIR.FIFOS_ENABLED` has to report `0x0`, the encoding `uart_16550_main.rdl` gives that state, and `LSR.DR` has to read clear, because the character parked in the FIFO is no longer on the path software reads. Two further characters are then sent one at a time and compared, which is the only traffic in the package that goes through the receiver buffer register rather than the FIFO.
* `programming.adoc` lists "Select DMA mode if using DMA transfers" as a step of FIFO configuration and `uart_16550_main_wo.rdl` gives FCR a `DMA_MODE_SELECT` bit. With DMA mode 1 selected and `FCR.RCVR_TRIGGER` at `0x1`, which that RDL names as four characters, one character is sent and left unread. Its trigger level is not reached, so the only interrupt that can arise is the reception timeout `architecture.adoc` fixes at four character times and `uart_16550_main.rdl` names id `0x6`; IIR has to report exactly that, and none pending once the character is read out. Four characters are then sent and read back in order, which is the same mode with the trigger level reached instead of timed out.
* `programming.adoc`: "With `MCR.LINE_LOOPBACK` set, the modem inputs are routed straight back out to the modem outputs and all four MSR level bits read `0`." The leaf sets it and requires those four bits to read 0. The receiver input is held idle in that mode, so a character written into THR has to be transmitted without ever being received: the leaf polls until `LSR.TEMT` reports the transmitter drained and requires `LSR.DR` clear on every read up to that point, which is a bound the DUT sets rather than a fixed wait.
* Still in line loopback, the divisor is slowed and a burst longer than `smc_config_pkg::UART_TX_FIFO_DEPTH` is written into THR without polling, so the transmit FIFO reaches its full condition while the transmitter is still draining. What the leaf checks is what the register map exposes: `LSR.THRE` clear straight after the burst, so the FIFO is holding characters, and `LSR.TEMT` and `LSR.THRE` both set once it has drained. The full flag is not a register field and is not claimed.
* Line loopback runs on wrapper 0 only. That mode substitutes the receive pin onto the transmit pin, and `hw/sys/smc/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` drives only UART0's receive pad -- idle high -- unless `+smc_uart_cross_3to0` shorts the pairs, so wrapper 0 is the only one where the substituted pin has a defined value. The other two modes use `MCR.LOOP`, which keeps every character inside the UART, and run on all four wrappers.
* Every character the legs send is distinct and the sequence fails if they are not, so a character one leg left behind cannot be read as the character another leg sent. The UART is returned to its reset configuration on each wrapper afterwards.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_uart_core_mode_select_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-UART-RBR-PATH` |On every wrapper FCR.FIFO_ENABLE was written clear with a character unread in the receive FIFO, IIR reported the FIFOs off and LSR stopped reporting received data, and the characters sent with the FIFOs off came back out of the receiver buffer register unchanged before the FIFOs were enabled again and IIR reported them on
|`CHK-UART-DMA-MODE-1` |On every wrapper FCR selected DMA mode 1 with the receive trigger at four characters, and every character sent in that mode came back in order, both below the trigger level and with it reached
|`CHK-UART-RX-TIMEOUT` |On every wrapper a single character left unread below the trigger level made IIR report an interrupt pending with the Reception Timeout id, the only source that can be pending below the trigger, and none pending once that character was read out
|`CHK-UART-LINE-LOOPBACK` |With MCR.LINE_LOOPBACK set on wrapper 0 all four MSR level bits read 0 as the programming guide requires, a character written into THR was transmitted to completion without the receiver ever reporting data, and a burst longer than the transmit FIFO left LSR reporting the holding register non-empty and then drained to both TEMT and THRE set
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-UART-RBR-PATH`, `CHK-UART-DMA-MODE-1`, `CHK-UART-RX-TIMEOUT`, `CHK-UART-LINE-LOOPBACK` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-212-smc_i2c_config_regblock_sweep_test]]
===== 212 `smc_i2c_config_regblock_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_config_regblock_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the I2C block left idle, neither engine enabled

*Objective*: Cycle every I2C configuration register on every instance with the block held idle.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_i2c_intr_reg_sweep_test` drives the interrupt and event registers and the protocol leaves drive the bus, but nothing writes the block's configuration. The control register, the five timing registers, both FIFO threshold registers, the three timeout controls, the NACK handler timeout, the target identity, the bus override and the SMBus control have never been written on any of the three instances, so their write enables and the read mux behind them have never been exercised.
* Each is driven to all-ones and all-zeros over the fields the generated contract pins, through half-register writes whose byte lanes over the other half are deasserted, and restored to its RDL reset. Every readback checks the whole register, so each half write also proves the untouched half kept its value.
* **The block stays idle.** `CTRL.ENABLEHOST` and `CTRL.ENABLETARGET` are the two fields that start the controller and the target, and both are held at their reset for the whole cycle. Every other CTRL field only shapes how a running block behaves. The bus monitor `CTRL.MULTI_CONTROLLER_MONITOR_EN` selects is a monitor whose outputs are detect and event strobes, nothing that drives SCL or SDA, and CTRL is cycled first so it is back at its reset before the timeout registers are given values.
* **The override never pulls a line down.** `i2c.rdl` gives `OVRD.SCLVAL` and `OVRD.SDAVAL` the sense "0 - Pull the line low, 1 - Release the line", and `TXOVRDEN` enables both. All three sit in one byte, so a single write moves them together: the ones pattern releases both lines while enabling the override, and the zero pattern clears `TXOVRDEN` in the same write that clears the values.
* **No zero the sweep writes is a value the reset does not already hold.** `i2c.rdl` says of `TIMING0.THIGH` and `TIMING0.TLOW` that each "Must be `>= 2`". Every register in the set resets to zero over the fields the cycle drives, so the all-zeros leg only restores what reset already put there; the constraint is a programming rule for a running controller, and this leaf never enables one.
* All three guarantees are checked as assertions over every word the cycle would leave resident -- the halves between the two writes of a leg included -- and they run before the sequence issues a single access, so a contract that changed under the sweep stops it rather than starting an engine or pulling a bus line low.
* `FIFO_CTRL` is not in the set: the generated contract gives it no software-writable field with a pinned readback, because `RXRST`, `FMTRST`, `ACQRST` and `TXRST` are every one of them self-clearing. The FIFO data ports and the status registers are excluded for the same reason, the engine writing only fields the contract pins exactly.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_config_regblock_sweep_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-CONFIG-REG-SWEEP` |All three I2C instances drove all fifteen configuration registers to all-ones and all-zeros over the fields their RDL pins, through half-register writes whose byte lanes over the other half were deasserted, and every register was restored to its reset and read back
|`CHK-I2C-CONFIG-IDLE-GUARD` |Every register cycle was checked before any access that the words it would leave resident hold CTRL.ENABLEHOST and CTRL.ENABLETARGET at their reset, never enable the bus override with SCLVAL or SDAVAL clear, and are zero over the driven fields wherever the reset is
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-I2C-CONFIG-IDLE-GUARD`, `CHK-I2C-CONFIG-REG-SWEEP` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-213-smc_i2c_fifo_reset_pulse_test]]
===== 213 `smc_i2c_fifo_reset_pulse_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_fifo_reset_pulse_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the I2C block left idle, neither engine enabled

*Objective*: Pulse each of the four I2C FIFO resets and check it against a measured fill level.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_i2c_config_regblock_sweep_test` cycles the I2C configuration registers but cannot express `FIFO_CTRL`: `i2c.rdl` makes all four of its fields `sw = w` with `singlepulse`, so the generated contract pins no written value to read back and the half-register cycle has nothing to drive. Nothing else writes the register either, so none of the four resets had been pulsed on any instance.
* The block is idle throughout -- `CTRL.ENABLEHOST` and `CTRL.ENABLETARGET` stay at their reset -- so no engine drains what the sequence puts in and every level read is one the sequence caused.
* **`FMTRST` and `TXRST` carry the strong claim.** `i2c.rdl` maps them to the Controller TX and Target TX FIFOs, and both of those have a software write port, `FDATA` and `TXDATA`, whose push is not gated on either engine enable. The sequence fills each, reads the level out of `HOST_FIFO_STATUS.FMTLVL` and `TARGET_FIFO_STATUS.TXLVL`, pulses the reset and requires that level to have gone to empty. The two fills are different depths -- three bytes and five -- so a status register reporting the other FIFO's level fails the compare before any reset is pulsed.
* **`RXRST` and `ACQRST` carry a weaker claim, stated here rather than implied.** They reset the Controller RX and Target RX FIFOs, and both of those are filled only by traffic on the wire: the controller receiving from a remote target, or a remote controller writing to this block as a target. Neither is reachable from the CSR port alone, and the leaves that drive the bus own the protocol agent and are a different shape. So what this leaf shows for these two is that each is pulsed while the two software-fillable FIFOs hold known, different levels and **neither level moves**. That fails if the reset decode is not one-hot, but it does **not** show that either reset empties the FIFO it names. Closing that needs a directed leaf on the protocol branch that first acquires a byte.
* The same one-hot check covers the other two: `FMTRST` must leave the target transmit level untouched while clearing the controller one, and `TXRST` is pulsed last with the controller FIFO already empty, so each of the four resets is shown to reach its own FIFO and no other.
* `FIFO_CTRL` is read after the four pulses and must read 0, which is the `singlepulse` contract the RDL gives all four fields.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_fifo_reset_pulse_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-FIFO-RST-EMPTIES` |On every instance FMTRST and TXRST each emptied the FIFO the RDL names for it, from a level the sequence had put there through FDATA and TXDATA and measured in the status registers first
|`CHK-I2C-FIFO-RST-ONE-HOT` |On every instance each of the four resets reached its own FIFO and no other -- RXRST and ACQRST left both measured levels where they were, FMTRST left the target transmit level untouched, TXRST cleared the target level with the controller FIFO already empty -- and FIFO_CTRL read 0 afterwards, which is the singlepulse contract for all four
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-I2C-FIFO-RST-EMPTIES`, `CHK-I2C-FIFO-RST-ONE-HOT` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-214-smc_filter_region_span_test]]
===== 214 `smc_filter_region_span_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_filter_region_span_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI, SEP_IN and JTAG); the SMC DMA as the outbound master

*Objective*: Program a filter region spanning two granules on every outbound entry, and show one of them enforced on the bus.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_filter_config_field_sweep_test` cycles `FILTER_CONFIG` on every entry and `smc_filter_multi_entry_test` reads the reset of all of them, but nothing programs `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR` into a region. A sweep cannot: its ones and zeros patterns give both registers the same value, so the start and the end always land in the same granule. Only `smc_dma_sanity_test_seq` programs a spanning region, and only on entry 0, which is why entries 1 to 15 had never had a start and an end in different granules.
* **The outbound filter carries the region, and the choice is from the document.** `hw/sys/smc/doc/fabric.adoc` separates the two: inbound filtering "protects SMC resources from unauthorized external access", which is the path this sequence's own CSR accesses arrive on, while outbound filtering "manages which external resources the SMC can access, restricting SMC-initiated requests". No access this leaf makes to program or read a filter can therefore be caught by the filter it is programming. The same document records the difference the traffic leg relies on: of traffic matching no entry, "the inbound filter blocks it, and the outbound filter permits it".
* **What the region proves comes from the RDL.** `filter_ctrl.rdl` states the write-back rule for both address registers: "Hardware writes the rounded start_addr and end_addr back into these fields only when both land in the same granule; otherwise readback returns the programmed values even though matching is still granule-aligned." Each of entries 1 to 15 is driven both ways and both readbacks are required -- a single-granule region must read back rounded down to the granule base and up to its top, a spanning one exactly as programmed -- so the leaf fails if hardware rounds a spanning region or leaves a single-granule one alone. `FILTER_CONFIG.allow_burst` is set, which that RDL makes the 4 KiB granule.
* **The enforcement leg uses the DMA, which is the outbound master.** `fabric.adoc` lists the DMA controller among the SMC-initiated paths that run "Alias remap -> filtering -> destination". Entry 1 is enabled over the spanning region with `write_allowed` clear and `read_allowed` set, and the DMA copies a word into the region and a word a granule above it. The copy inside must leave its destination holding the sentinel it was seeded with; the copy outside must land, and that second copy is what shows the first was refused rather than the DMA idle. Both descriptors are required to complete, so neither result can come from a transfer that never ran.
* The observable there is memory rather than the response, and deliberately: the backend is elaborated with `ErrorCap(idma_pkg::NO_ERROR_HANDLING)`, so a refused write raises no error the DMA reports. The JTAG2AXI bridge seeds and reads those addresses and is not the master under test -- a first attempt drove the traffic through it and no refusal appeared, because that bridge enters the fabric on the inbound side rather than the outbound one the entry sits on.
* Entry 1 is the highest-priority entry `smc_dma_sanity_test_seq` leaves alone, the hit index being a leading-zero count over the entries. The leaf reads entry 0 first and fails if it is already enabled, so a rule of somebody else's cannot be what the traffic leg measures.
* Every entry the leaf programs is restored to its RDL reset, the traffic entry's three registers are read back to prove it, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` is never written.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_filter_region_span_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-FILTER-REGION-SPAN` |Each of outbound entries 1 to 15 took a region whose start and end sit in different 4 KiB granules and read both address registers back exactly as programmed, and a region inside one granule and read it back rounded to the granule base and top, which is the write-back rule filter_ctrl.rdl states
|`CHK-FILTER-REGION-ENFORCED` |With outbound entry 1 enabled over a two-granule region with write_allowed clear, a DMA copy into the region left its destination at the seeded sentinel while a copy a granule above the region landed, both transfers having completed; every entry was restored to its RDL reset and read back and the write-once lock was never written
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-FILTER-REGION-ENFORCED`, `CHK-FILTER-REGION-SPAN` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-215-smc_dma_decouple_rw_test]]
===== 215 `smc_dma_decouple_rw_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_dma_decouple_rw_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI, SEP_IN and JTAG); the SMC DMA

*Objective*: Run DMA transfers with the read and write channels decoupled, and queue a second descriptor while the first is in flight.

*Test Coverage*:

* `CONFIG.DECOUPLE_RW` has never been written. Every DMA leaf leaves it at its reset, so the backend legalizer has only ever run its coupled arm, where the read and the write machine advance together; the decoupled arm, where either may advance alone, has never been entered. Nothing has queued a second descriptor while the legalizer was still busy either.
* The golden for the field is the generated header, not the RTL: `dma_ctrl.h` gives `DMA_CTRL__CONFIG__DECOUPLE_RW_bm` the value `0x2` and its reset `0x0`, and `dma_ctrl.rdl` makes it `sw = rw`. The leaf reads `CONFIG` first and fails if the field is already set, writes it, and requires it to read back before running anything through it.
* **Two transfers whose channels have different work to do.** Decoupling only shows if the two sides are not in step, so each transfer is two-dimensional with a stride on one side only: a scatter, where the source rows are contiguous and the destination rows are a stride apart, so the read side issues one long run and the write side many short ones; and a gather, the other way round, so the fragmented side is the read. Both move the same four distinct rows and every row is compared against its source at the stride that should have placed it, so a transfer that dropped, duplicated or misplaced a row fails rather than passing quietly.
* **Two descriptors back to back.** `dma_ctrl.rdl` says of `NEXT_ID` that "Reading this register starts the DMA transfer. Returns an ID value for the cumulative number of transfers. Returns 0 if command was not set up correctly", and of `DONE` that it "Holds the cumulative number of completed transfers". The second descriptor is therefore programmed and submitted without waiting for the first, and the leaf requires both ids to be non-zero and rising, `DONE` to have advanced by two, and each destination to hold its own payload rather than the other's or the sentinel.
* Every destination is seeded with a sentinel that is none of the payload rows, so a transfer that never ran reads as the sentinel and fails.
* `CONFIG` is restored to its reset and read back. The leaf writes no other DMA field, and touches no fuse.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_dma_decouple_rw_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DMA-DECOUPLE-RW` |CONFIG.DECOUPLE_RW read back set from the mask the generated header gives it, and both transfers ran with the channels decoupled -- a scatter whose write side was the fragmented one and a gather whose read side was -- with every row landing where its stride puts it and matching its source; CONFIG was restored to its reset and read back
|`CHK-DMA-QUEUED-DESCRIPTORS` |A second descriptor was programmed and submitted without waiting for the first, both NEXT_ID reads returned a non-zero and rising id rather than the 0 the RDL gives a rejected command, DONE advanced by two, and each destination held its own payload
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-DMA-DECOUPLE-RW`, `CHK-DMA-QUEUED-DESCRIPTORS` hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-216-smc_intr_status_partial_clear_test]]
===== 216 `smc_intr_status_partial_clear_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_intr_status_partial_clear_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Clear write-one-to-clear status bits a few at a time and show the rest survive the write.

*Test Coverage*:

* Three blocks carry an interrupt status register whose bits software raises through the matching `INTR_TEST` and clears by writing a one back: the I2C `INTR_STATE`, the log-engine `INTR_STATUS` and the telemetry-receiver `INTR_STATUS`. Every leaf that touches them raises the whole declared mask and clears the whole declared mask, so no write has ever carried a zero over a bit that was set, and the half of the `oneToClear` contract that says such a bit *stays* set has never been exercised on any instance.
* On each of the ten instances the leaf reads the register and requires it clear, so every bit it goes on to see set is one it raised itself; writes the event bits of `INTR_TEST` and requires exactly those raised; writes back half of them and requires precisely that half to have gone and the other half to still be set; then writes the rest and requires the register to read clear again, which is its reset.
* **Only the `read-write` fields take part, and the reason is in the generated map.** A status register mixes two kinds of field. The event bits are `read-write` -- hardware sets them, software clears them with a one, and they hold otherwise. The rest are `read-only` level bits hardware drives from a condition, and on I2C those are the FIFO thresholds and the stretch and halt indications; a write cannot clear one of those while its condition holds, so the leaf excludes them by their access type rather than expecting them to go. A first version did not, and five I2C bits -- `FMT_THRESHOLD`, `ACQ_THRESHOLD`, `CONTROLLER_HALT`, `TX_STRETCH` and `ACQ_STRETCH` -- correctly refused to clear.
* The two halves are alternating bits of the mask, so neither is a contiguous run a byte-granular decode could clear by accident. Where a register has only one clearable bit the write that has to leave it set carries no bits at all, which puts the same contract to the same test.
* Raising a status bit through `INTR_TEST` is the mechanism `smc_i2c_intr_reg_sweep_test` already uses. Nothing is left raised: every register ends its leg reading clear.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_intr_status_partial_clear_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-INTR-STATUS-PARTIAL-CLEAR` |On all ten write-one-to-clear status registers across the I2C blocks, the log engines and the telemetry receivers, the event mask was raised through INTR_TEST, half of it was written back and went, the bits the write carried a zero over stayed set, and the register read clear again once the rest was written
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-INTR-STATUS-PARTIAL-CLEAR` holds after its compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-217-smc_gpio_data_ctrl_field_test]]
===== 217 `smc_gpio_data_ctrl_field_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_gpio_data_ctrl_field_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Drive each software-writable DATA_CTRL field of one idle GPIO instance on its own.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_gpio_intf_regblock_sweep_test` cycles `DATA_CTRL_ENABLE` and `ACCESS_FILTER` on every GPIO_INTF instance but leaves `DATA_CTRL` at its reset word, because writing it moves the pad mux and can drive the pad. The write side of its seven software-writable fields had therefore never run.
* The footprint is the smallest that reaches them: **one instance, one field at a time, each field set and put straight back to its reset with a read-back on either side, and nothing held between fields.** At no point is more than one field off its reset value, and the register is read against its RDL reset before the first field and after the last.
* **Which instance, and why it is safe.** `smc_padring.sv` gives a function to every index except 13, 14, 17, 18, 21, 22, 25, 26, 52, 59 and 60 -- SPI takes 0-10, the UARTs 11, 12, 15, 16, 19, 20, 23 and 24, I3C 27-36, I2C 37-48, and the named pads 49-51, 53-58 and 61-64 carry boot, OCCP, cool reset and the rest. Index 60 is in none of them; `tb_top.sv` never names it, so the bench neither drives nor observes it; and the only `DATA_CTRL` index any other leaf touches is 0. The leaf also reads the register first and fails if it is not at its reset, so an instance somebody else had taken would stop it.
* **What the pad does during the cycle: nothing.** `gpio.sv` builds the output enable as `lsio_pin ? ~lsio_core2pad_en_ni : sel_reg_tx ? enable_rx_tx[0] : lsio_sw ? ~lsio_core2pad_en_ni : 1'b0`, with `lsio_pin = lsio_interface_select_i && ~lsio_disable` and `sel_reg_tx = interface_enable || use_reg_tx`. For index 60 the padring leaves both LSIO inputs at the defaults it assigns at the top of its comb block -- `lsio_interface_select_o = '0` and `lsio_core2pad_en_n = DISABLED`, which `smc_padring_pkg` defines as `1'b1` -- so `lsio_pin` is 0 and both LSIO branches give an output enable of 0. The only branch that can drive the pad is `sel_reg_tx && enable_rx_tx[0]`, and that needs two fields off their reset at once: `enable_rx_tx` together with either `interface_enable` or `DATA_CTRL_ENABLE.use_reg_tx`. One field at a time is exactly what makes that combination unreachable, so the output enable is 0 at every step and the pad stays in its reset drive state throughout.
* The order still puts the fields that cannot reach the pad mux first -- `interrupt_enable`, `interrupt_type`, `core2pad`, `enable_rx_tx` -- then `lsio_disable`, which only ever withdraws LSIO ownership, then `lsio_select` and `interface_enable` last.
* The set of fields the leaf drives is compared against the generated contract, so a regenerated map that adds or removes a software-writable field stops the leaf rather than silently skipping it.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_gpio_data_ctrl_field_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-GPIO-DATA-CTRL-FIELDS` |On GPIO instance 60 each of the seven software-writable DATA_CTRL fields was set on its own and put straight back to its reset, with the whole register read after every write, no bit outside the declared fields driven, and the register at its RDL reset before the first field and after the last
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-GPIO-DATA-CTRL-FIELDS` holds after its compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-218-smc_alias_remap_region_attrs_test]]
===== 218 `smc_alias_remap_region_attrs_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_alias_remap_region_attrs_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI)

*Objective*: Mark one alias-remap region cacheable and valid, and hold both across a write that does not select them.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smc_alias_remap_spare_block_decode_test` writes `REGION_START` on all eight regions and restores it, but nothing writes `REGION_ATTRS`. No region has ever been marked cacheable or valid, so neither attribute bit has ever held a one while a write arrived that did not select its byte lanes.
* **The region cannot match an address, by its own reset.** `alias_remap.rdl` makes `REGION_END` "End of remap region (non-inclusive)", and the generated map gives both `REGION_START` and `REGION_END` the reset `0x0`. A region whose non-inclusive end equals its start contains no address, so the leaf never writes either register: it leaves the window empty and touches only `REGION_ATTRS`. It reads both back first and fails if they are not equal, so a window somebody else had opened would stop it. That is a stronger guarantee than choosing an address range believed to be unused, and it holds the footprint to one register.
* **`valid` is an identity remap even so.** `REGION_ATTRS.offset` is "added to bits [55:12] of the input address when it falls within the remap region", and the leaf leaves it at its reset of zero, checking that in the guard read. So even if the empty window could match, the remap would add nothing.
* **Nothing else is remapping.** All eight regions are read first and every one must have `valid` clear, so the region the leaf marks valid is the only one in the block and no rule of somebody else's can be what the readbacks report.
* The two attribute bits are then set together, the register is read back as a guard before anything depends on it, and **only the low four bytes** are written -- the half `offset` occupies, with the byte lanes over bits 62 and 63 deasserted. Both attribute bits have to still be set, which is the retain behaviour of a field a write did not select, and the offset has to remain zero. `REGION_ATTRS` is restored to its reset and read back.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_alias_remap_region_attrs_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-ATTRS-RETAIN` |All eight alias-remap regions read with valid clear, region 7 was marked cacheable and valid over a window its own reset leaves empty and with the remap offset at zero, both attribute bits survived a four-byte write of the half of the register they do not occupy, and the register was restored to its reset and read back
|===

*Pass Criteria*: `CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-ATTRS-RETAIN` holds after its compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-220-smc_avsbus_frame_transaction_test]]
===== 220 `smc_avsbus_frame_transaction_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_avsbus_frame_transaction_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the AVSBus sdata pad held at the responding level by the bench

*Objective*: Three queued AVS_CMD words walk the AVSBus command path against a responding pad and land one readback entry each.

*Test Coverage*:

* `AVS_CFG_1.STOP_AVS_CLOCK_ON_IDLE` routes the first launch through a slave resync, so the command path is entered the same way on every run.
* Three commands queued back to back exercise the first, mid and last subframe shapes in one burst.
* The sdata pad is held low, which `interface.adoc` "AVSBus Slave Subframe" makes a valid frame with slave acknowledgment 0, so the controller takes the accept path rather than the retry path.
* The single-cycle end-of-subframe and post-resync launch states are not directly observable: `cur_state_debug_o` reaches the bench through two handshake data synchronizers, which forward the value they hold when a transfer opens. Their effect is checked instead.
* The slave response word is not compared: the readback register reads 0 both for the all-zero subframe and for an empty FIFO.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_avsbus_frame_transaction_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AVS-FRAME-FSM-MAIN-PATH` |`tb_avsbus_cur_state_debug` held each of AVS_IDLE, AVS_SLAVE_RESYNC, AVS_SHIFT_1ST_SUBFRAME, AVS_SHIFT_MID_SUBFRAME and AVS_SHIFT_LAST_SUBFRAME over two consecutive `clk_periph_i` samples; the one-hot bit order is transcribed from `architecture.adoc` "Protocol State Machine"
|`CHK-AVS-MASTER-SUBFRAME` |Every master subframe captured on `avs_mdata_o` over the DUT's own AVS clock equals the queued AVS_CMD word in wire order -- `2'b01` preamble in [31:30] and the generated-header AVS_CMD fields in [29:3]; the hardware CRC-3 is excluded because no source in this tree publishes the polynomial
|`CHK-AVS-CMD-FIFO-DRAIN` |After all three responses, AVS_FIFOS_STATUS reports the command FIFO at 0 occupied and the full generated depth vacant, with AVS_NORMAL_STATUS CMD_FIFO_EMPTY and AVS_BUS_IS_IDLE set
|`CHK-AVS-READBACK-FIFO-FILL` |AVS_FIFOS_STATUS reports exactly one readback entry per command and the matching vacant count, with AVS_NORMAL_STATUS READBACK_HAS_DATA set
|`CHK-AVS-READBACK-POP` |Each AVS_READBACK read returned OKAY on a non-empty FIFO and advanced the occupancy by exactly one, ending at 0 with READBACK_HAS_DATA clear
|`CHK-AVS-VALID-ACK-NO-RETRY` |TOTAL_RETRIES is 0, AVS_MASTER_IS_RETRYING is clear, and neither SLAVE_UNRESPONSIVE_INT nor MAX_RETRIES_ATTEMPTED_INT is raised; `smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test` drives all three to 1 on the same registers
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all six checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-221-smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test]]
===== 221 `smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the AVSBus sdata pad held at its idle level by the bench

*Objective*: Two unanswered AVS_CMD words walk the AVSBus retry states and exhaust the programmed retry budget.

*Test Coverage*:

* The sdata pad idles high, which `interface.adoc` "AVSBus Slave Subframe" makes a frame whose Frame Valid bit says the target did not respond.
* `AVS_CFG_0.MAX_RETRIES` is programmed to 2, so the retry countdown is walked rather than stepped straight to zero.
* Two commands queued back to back separate the retry shapes: the reply to the first arrives while the second is on the wire, so the controller retries while still receiving before alternating transmit-only and receive-only retries.
* The single-cycle end-of-subframe and buffered-reply retry states are not directly observable through the handshake-synchronised debug bus; the interrupt and retry-count checks stand in for them.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AVS-RETRY-FSM-PATH` |`tb_avsbus_cur_state_debug` held each of AVS_RETRY_SHIFT_XMIT_AND_RECV_SUBFRAME, AVS_RETRY_SHIFT_XMIT_SUBFRAME and AVS_RETRY_SHIFT_RECV_SUBFRAME over two consecutive `clk_periph_i` samples
|`CHK-AVS-RETRY-IN-PROGRESS` |AVS_NORMAL_STATUS.AVS_MASTER_IS_RETRYING read back set on at least one poll taken while the burst was in flight, a register-path witness independent of the debug bus
|`CHK-AVS-RETRY-BUDGET-EXHAUSTED` |AVS_NORMAL_STATUS.TOTAL_RETRIES reached at least the programmed budget and AVS_INTERRUPT carries both SLAVE_UNRESPONSIVE_INT and MAX_RETRIES_ATTEMPTED_INT
|`CHK-AVS-RETRY-INT-W1C` |On an idle bus, AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR with both clear bits returned AVS_INTERRUPT to neither flag raised
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all four checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-222-smc_telemetry_atb_capture_test]]
===== 222 `smc_telemetry_atb_capture_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_telemetry_atb_capture_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the bench ATB beat driver on the receiver 0 pins

*Objective*: One ATB message captured on telemetry receiver 0, read back through its registers, then the receiver and transmitter flush paths.

*Test Coverage*:

* Follows the three procedures in `hw/ip/telemetry_receiver/doc/programming.adoc`: reading a message, receiver flush and transmitter flush.
* Each ATB beat requires the `atready_o` handshake; expiry is a failure, because a truncated frame can still clear STATUS.BUFFER_EMPTY and still match the probe id.
* Which 9-bit block a counter byte lands in has no documented source, so every block below the header is filled valid and all-ones. `doc/index.adoc` supplies the rest: seven blocks per packet, four consecutive blocks per counter, and a counter valid only when all four of its blocks are.
* The flush-acknowledge pins exist for receiver 0 only, so the transmitter-flush leg is not repeated on receivers 1 and 2.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_telemetry_atb_capture_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-TELEMETRY-ATB-MESSAGE-CAPTURE` |A complete last-flagged message cleared STATUS.BUFFER_EMPTY, TELEMETRY_PROBE_ID read back the framed probe id, TELEMETRY_COUNTER_VLDS read exactly one valid counter, TELEMETRY_COUNTER[0] read the all-ones value reassembled from four valid blocks while TELEMETRY_COUNTER[1] stayed at its generated reset, and CTRL.BUFFER_POP returned the buffer to empty
|`CHK-TELEMETRY-RX-FLUSH` |With a message buffered, CTRL.TELEMETRY_RX_FLUSH returned STATUS.BUFFER_EMPTY to 1 within the poll bound and clearing the request read CTRL back at 0
|`CHK-TELEMETRY-TX-FLUSH-HANDSHAKE` |`afvalid_o` idled at 0 with `afready_i` low, rose after CTRL.TELEMETRY_TX_FLUSH was written and stayed asserted while `afready_i` was low, then retired once `afready_i` went high with CTRL.TELEMETRY_TX_FLUSH self-cleared
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-223-smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test]]
===== 223 `smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the open-drain I2C0 pads

*Objective*: The I2C0 target holds SCL low once its acquisition FIFO has no room, and releases it when software drains ACQDATA.

*Test Coverage*:

* The DUT's own I2C0 block is the addressed target; the bench is the controller on the pads, so the whole transaction crosses the SMC boundary.
* The controller writes more payload bytes than the acquisition FIFO holds and nothing drains ACQDATA, which is the only way to reach the full condition from outside.
* The acquisition FIFO depth is measured from TARGET_FIFO_STATUS.ACQLVL rather than assumed.
* The hold is measured, not inferred: only the samples where the resolved SCL net is low while the bench has released its own pull are counted, and nothing but the target can pull SCL low then.
* `CTRL.ACQ_START_STOP_EN` stays at its reset, so the acquisition FIFO carries the payload bytes with no start or stop entry around them.
* Holding SCL through the address phase of a later transaction is not driven: it needs the acquisition FIFO to still be full when that transaction starts, and this bench can only end the current transaction by freeing the room.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ACQ-STRETCH` |With the controller writing and nothing draining, I2C0 STATUS.ACQFULL set with a non-zero TARGET_FIFO_STATUS.ACQLVL, and the resolved SCL net was low on at least the minimum number of samples taken while the bench had released its own pull
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ACQ-DRAIN` |Reading ACQDATA released the hold and let the controller's write finish with every byte acknowledged -- the bench controller raises on a NACK of the address or of any data byte -- and the acquired entries are exactly the payload bytes in order, leaving STATUS.ACQFULL clear and STATUS.ACQEMPTY set
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-224-smc_i2c_target_multi_instance_stretch_test]]
===== 224 `smc_i2c_target_multi_instance_stretch_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_multi_instance_stretch_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); one DUT I2C instance as the controller and another as the target, on the shared open-drain bus

*Objective*: I2C1 and I2C2 as targets: the transmit path, an unmatched address, and both the transmit-FIFO and acquisition-FIFO stretches.

*Test Coverage*:

* `+smc_i2c_shared_bus` puts all three instances on one open-drain bus, so the enabled controller reaches either target by address. Both ends of every check are DUT instances.
* I2C0 is the controller throughout, which leaves the target-side paths to I2C1 and I2C2 -- the instances that have never seen them. I2C2 in particular has only ever been written to as a target.
* Each target gets an address of its own and sources a byte of its own, so a readback cannot be satisfied by the other target answering.
* The transmit leg runs with `CTRL.TX_STRETCH_CTRL_EN` set and the transmit FIFO empty, so the target has to hold the bus and raise `TARGET_EVENTS.TX_PENDING` before software supplies the byte.
* The acquisition-stretch hold is measured through the controller rather than on the bus: both ends are DUT instances, so a low SCL net does not say which is holding it. `smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test` is the bench-controller variant on I2C0, where only the target can be pulling SCL low.
* The acquisition FIFO depth is read from `TARGET_FIFO_STATUS.ACQLVL` rather than assumed, and the last payload byte carries the STOP so the transaction cannot end while the FIFO is still full.
* Address-phase stretch (`StretchAddr`, `StretchAddrAck`, `StretchAddrAckSetup`) stays out: it needs the acquisition FIFO still full when a later transaction starts, and `nack_addr_after_timeout` besides, which no register this bench programs reaches.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_target_multi_instance_stretch_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C1-TGT-TX-STRETCH` |On a read with `CTRL.TX_STRETCH_CTRL_EN` set and its transmit FIFO empty, I2C1 raised `TARGET_EVENTS.TX_PENDING` and held the transfer until software wrote TXDATA
|`CHK-I2C1-TGT-READ` |I2C1 sourced the byte written to its TXDATA and the controller read exactly that byte back, then returned idle with its format FIFO drained
|`CHK-I2C2-TGT-TX-STRETCH` |The same transmit-FIFO hold on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-TGT-READ` |The same sourced-byte readback on I2C2, which is its first target-side transmit
|`CHK-I2C2-TGT-ADDR-MISMATCH` |An address no enabled target matches left I2C2's acquisition FIFO empty and the controller reporting `CONTROLLER_EVENTS.NACK`; the transmit leg on the same instance is the matching-address control
|`CHK-I2C1-TGT-ACQ-STRETCH` |With nothing draining ACQDATA, I2C1 set `STATUS.ACQFULL` with a non-zero `TARGET_FIFO_STATUS.ACQLVL`, and on every poll taken while it stayed set the controller was still busy with queued format entries
|`CHK-I2C1-TGT-ACQ-DRAIN` |Reading ACQDATA released the controller, which then completed the write; I2C1 acquired every payload byte in order and `STATUS.ACQFULL` is clear
|`CHK-I2C2-TGT-ACQ-STRETCH` |The same acquisition-FIFO hold on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-TGT-ACQ-DRAIN` |The same release and byte-stream compare on I2C2
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all nine checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-225-smc_i2c_protocol_interruption_test]]
===== 225 `smc_i2c_protocol_interruption_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_protocol_interruption_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the shared open-drain I2C bus

*Objective*: Transactions cut short by a STOP, a repeated START or a cleared enable at every phase, on all three instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* Nearly every state of the target state machine has an edge back to `Idle`, to `WaitForStop` and to `AcquireStart`, because a STOP, a repeated START or a cleared enable can arrive in any of them. Reaching those edges needs the interruption at a chosen bit slot, which only the bench controller can drive: a DUT controller cannot be told to stop in the middle of a byte.
* Slots come from the VIP's own bit timing. A slot is one call to its `send_bit`, and the interruption is its own `send_stop` or `send_start` issued from wherever the loop stopped.
* Address-phase injections land before the target matches, so nothing is acquired; the claim there is that the instance is still able to run a clean transaction.
* Data-phase injections land after the address was acknowledged, so each leaves a START entry in the acquisition FIFO, checked per injection rather than as a total.
* The acknowledge slots are injected after rather than during. The target owns SDA while it acknowledges, so a controller physically cannot release SDA for a STOP in the middle of one; attempting it wedges the bus rather than testing anything.
* Every leg opens and closes with a clean transaction. The opening one is the positive control -- without it a sweep against an unreachable instance would satisfy every "nothing was acquired" check vacuously -- and the closing one is the recovery proof.
* Each injection waits for the target to report itself between transactions before the next begins, so one transaction's tail cannot land in the next one's acquisition window.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_protocol_interruption_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C0-INTR-ADDR-PHASE` |14 transactions cut short at every address-byte slot, by STOP and by repeated START, left no payload byte acquired on I2C0, which ran a clean write before and after the sweep
|`CHK-I2C0-INTR-DATA-PHASE` |18 transactions cut short after the address acknowledge, at every payload-byte slot and after the payload acknowledge, each left at least one START entry in I2C0's acquisition FIFO, with a clean write before and after
|`CHK-I2C0-INTR-TARGET-DISABLE` |Clearing `CTRL` target enable at each of six delays spread across the address byte, the acknowledge slot and the payload byte left I2C0 reporting TARGETIDLE, and it ran a clean write again after each
|`CHK-I2C1-INTR-ADDR-PHASE` |The address-phase sweep on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C1-INTR-DATA-PHASE` |The data-phase sweep on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C1-INTR-TARGET-DISABLE` |The target-disable sweep on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C2-INTR-ADDR-PHASE` |The address-phase sweep on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-INTR-DATA-PHASE` |The data-phase sweep on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-INTR-TARGET-DISABLE` |The target-disable sweep on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C0-INTR-HOST-DISABLE` |Clearing `CTRL` host enable while a queued format-FIFO transaction was on the wire returned controller I2C0 to idle, and it then drove a clean write that the target acquired
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all ten checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-226-smc_i2c2_controller_rdwr_test]]
===== 226 `smc_i2c2_controller_rdwr_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c2_controller_rdwr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); I2C2 as the bus controller and I2C1 as the target

*Objective*: I2C2 driving the shared bus as controller for a write and a two-byte read against I2C1.

*Test Coverage*:

* Instance 2 has only ever been a target, so its controller state machine has never acknowledged a byte and never clocked one in. Which instance is the controller is a matter of which one gets `CTRL.ENABLEHOST`, so no new bench plumbing is needed.
* The read asks for two bytes rather than one. A controller reading a single byte only ever NACKs it, so the acknowledge phase is entered only when a byte is followed by another.
* Both legs are checked at the far end: the write against the bytes the target acquired, the read against the bytes the target was told to source.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c2_controller_rdwr_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C2-CTRL-WRITE` |Controller I2C2 drove the bus and target I2C1 acquired every payload byte in order, with no NACK reported in `CONTROLLER_EVENTS`
|`CHK-I2C2-CTRL-READ` |Controller I2C2 clocked two bytes out of target I2C1 and read back exactly the bytes written to the target's TXDATA, acknowledging every byte but the last, then returned idle
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-227-smc_i2c_target_bus_timeout_test]]
===== 227 `smc_i2c_target_bus_timeout_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_bus_timeout_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the shared open-drain I2C bus

*Objective*: The programmable bus timeout expiring at every slot of an addressed target transaction, and against the target's own stretch, on all three instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* `hw/ip/i2c/regs/i2c.rdl` describes `TIMEOUT_CTRL.MODE` 1 as a bus timeout that fires "if SCL is LOW for more time than `TIMEOUT_CTRL.VAL`", and `doc/architecture.adoc` adds that the count "accumulates across all sources" and that "the counter resets when SCL goes high, so this count only accumulates during a single bit transfer". Parking SCL low is therefore what makes it expire.
* Unlike a STOP or a repeated START, the timeout is a counter rather than a bus event, so it lands in states where no control symbol can be driven -- the acknowledge slots, and the states the target reaches while stretching.
* The timeout value sits well above the VIP's per-bit SCL low time and the deliberate park sits well above the value, so the timeout fires only where this leaf puts it and never on ordinary bit timing.
* The stretch leg drives nothing on the bus at all: the target is holding SCL low itself because its acquisition FIFO is full, and the timeout counts that hold.
* The address-stretch leg then starts a transaction while the acquisition FIFO is still full, so the address cannot be deposited. The filling write is abandoned first: it and the new transaction share one VIP and cannot be in flight together.
* `doc/architecture.adoc` states that a bus timeout while the target was addressed adds an entry to the ACQ FIFO, and the RDL defines `SIGNAL` 6 for a transaction that "ended abnormally, for example, due to ... a Bus or Stretch Timeout". That entry is the observable.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_target_bus_timeout_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C0-BUSTO-SLOT-SWEEP` |The bus timeout expired at each of nine slots of an addressed transaction on I2C0, every one recorded as an abnormal ending in the acquisition FIFO, with a clean write before and after
|`CHK-I2C0-BUSTO-STRETCH` |With its acquisition FIFO full, I2C0 held SCL low itself and the timeout counted that hold and returned the instance to idle, with nothing driven on the bus
|`CHK-I2C0-BUSTO-ADDR-STRETCH` |A transaction started while I2C0's acquisition FIFO was still full drove the address phase against a target with nowhere to deposit it, and the instance ran a clean write afterwards
|`CHK-I2C1-BUSTO-SLOT-SWEEP` |The slot sweep on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C1-BUSTO-STRETCH` |The stretch timeout on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C1-BUSTO-ADDR-STRETCH` |The address-phase stretch on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C2-BUSTO-SLOT-SWEEP` |The slot sweep on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-BUSTO-STRETCH` |The stretch timeout on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-BUSTO-ADDR-STRETCH` |The address-phase stretch on I2C2
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all nine checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-228-smc_i2c_bus_monitor_idle_test]]
===== 228 `smc_i2c_bus_monitor_idle_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_bus_monitor_idle_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the shared open-drain I2C bus

*Objective*: The I2C bus monitor's idle-with-SCL-high state entered and left on every instance, witnessed by the host-timeout interrupt.

*Test Coverage*:

* `HOST_TIMEOUT_CTRL.VAL` arms the bus monitor's inactive timeout, and the RDL calls the resulting interrupt a "Target Mode interrupt: asserted when the controller stops generating the clock longer than `HOST_TIMEOUT_CTRL.VAL`". Nothing else in this package programs that register.
* `INTR_STATE.HOST_TIMEOUT` is what makes the state observable rather than inferred: only the idling state counts that timeout down, so the interrupt firing is a witness that the monitor was in it.
* `TIMING4.T_BUF` is programmed long for this leaf, so the bus-free window after a STOP is wide enough for the bench to drive the bus low inside it; at the default of a few cycles it is shorter than a round trip from the sequence.
* The enable-with-multi-controller leg drives the monitor's other entry into the idling state, but asserts only that a clean transaction follows: the inactive counter is not reloaded on that path, so the host-timeout witness does not fire there, and no register reports the monitor's state directly. That entry is stimulus, not a claim.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_bus_monitor_idle_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C0-BUSMON-IDLE-TIMEOUT` |With the bus busy and the bench holding SCL released mid-transaction, I2C0's monitor counted `HOST_TIMEOUT_CTRL.VAL` down and raised `INTR_STATE.HOST_TIMEOUT`, with a clean write before and after
|`CHK-I2C0-BUSMON-RESUME` |I2C0's monitor left the idling state both ways -- SCL taken low again, and a STOP driven out of it followed by the bus pulled low inside the bus-free window -- and the instance ran a clean write after
|`CHK-I2C0-BUSMON-MULTI-ENABLE` |I2C0 enabled with `CTRL.MULTI_CONTROLLER_MONITOR_EN` set on a quiet bus then ran a clean write that was acquired byte for byte
|`CHK-I2C1-BUSMON-IDLE-TIMEOUT` |The idle timeout on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C1-BUSMON-RESUME` |The resume and stop legs on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C1-BUSMON-MULTI-ENABLE` |The multi-controller enable on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C2-BUSMON-IDLE-TIMEOUT` |The idle timeout on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-BUSMON-RESUME` |The resume and stop legs on I2C2
|`CHK-I2C2-BUSMON-MULTI-ENABLE` |The multi-controller enable on I2C2
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all nine checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-229-smc_i2c_controller_sda_interference_test]]
===== 229 `smc_i2c_controller_sda_interference_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_controller_sda_interference_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); one DUT I2C instance as the controller, another as the target, and `SmcI2cMasterVip` pulling SDA low from the pads

*Objective*: SDA pulled low under a transmitting I2C controller, on all three instances.

*Test Coverage*:

* `doc/architecture.adoc` Bus Arbitration describes the bus monitor detecting a controller that "attempts to transmit a logic high, but another device is pulling SDA low", and the RDL gives `INTR_STATE.SDA_INTERFERENCE` for the controller side. That condition is a global override on the controller state machine, so it returns the controller to idle from whatever state it was in -- unlike clearing the host enable, which is only acted on in particular states.
* Every payload byte is 0xFF, so the controller drives a logic high in every data slot and the pull-down is a genuine conflict rather than a bit that happened to agree.
* The injection is anchored to the controller leaving idle, so the delay is measured from something the DUT did, and is expressed in the bit period this leaf programmed into `TIMING0`.
* **One injection point per instance, not a sweep.** Where the pull-down lands decides which of two things the controller reports: at this point it reports the interference under test, while at a half-bit offset and at other whole-bit offsets the byte is corrupted early enough that the target NACKs first and the controller halts on the NACK instead. A longer pull-down did not change that, and the delay is only as accurate as the poll that observes the controller leaving idle, so a finer sweep would not place the conflict where it claimed to.
* **Recovery is not asserted.** After an interference event on this DUT the next transaction from the same controller is NACKed even when, measured immediately beforehand, the controller's events read 0 and it reports idle, the interrupted transaction has been ended with a STOP the target acknowledges by reporting itself idle, the target has been through disable and a full re-enable, and the bus reads released. The identical transaction succeeds as the opening control. Rather than encode an expectation around behaviour that is not understood, both instances are hard reset between injections and nothing is claimed about the transaction that follows an event.
* The opening clean transaction of each instance is the positive control: it shows the pair works, so an injection that reports nothing is a real absence rather than a bench that never reached the DUT.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_i2c_controller_sda_interference_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C0-CTRL-SDA-INTERFERENCE` |SDA pulled low under controller I2C0 while it drove a logic high raised `INTR_STATE.SDA_INTERFERENCE` and returned the controller to idle, with a clean write to its target beforehand as the control
|`CHK-I2C1-CTRL-SDA-INTERFERENCE` |The same on I2C1
|`CHK-I2C2-CTRL-SDA-INTERFERENCE` |The same on I2C2
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-230-smc_avsbus_interrupt_sources_test]]
===== 230 `smc_avsbus_interrupt_sources_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_avsbus_interrupt_sources_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the AVSBus sdata pad held at the responding level by the bench

*Objective*: Each AVSBus FIFO interrupt source raised, observed, cleared through the write-only clear register and observed gone.

*Test Coverage*:

* `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` is write-only and single-pulse, so writing a bit is only worth anything with the matching source already raised: a write with nothing pending clears nothing and proves nothing. The retry sources are covered by `smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test`; this leaf raises the FIFO sources.
* The command FIFO is filled with the AVS clock gated off, so nothing drains it while it fills and the full condition does not depend on winning a race against the protocol engine.
* Both full flags follow their conditions rather than latching, so each is cleared only after its FIFO has drained; clearing while the condition holds leaves the flag set, which an earlier version of this leaf failed on.
* The overflowing `AVS_CMD` write is refused on the bus as well as dropped, so the access itself is an observable of the full condition.
* FIFO depths come from the generated register header's reset values for the vacant-slot fields, not from the design.
* `READBACK_OVERFLOW_INT` is not raised here. `architecture.adoc` lists it under Error Handling as "a response received when full is dropped", while also stating that "while the readback FIFO is full the master launches no further commands". The two statements are hard to reconcile from the documentation alone, and the full flag crosses a clock domain, so a race that drops a response in flight is not ruled out. The source is left to a leaf that can set that race up deliberately.
* The interrupt output is read as a DUT-produced observable. At reset every source is masked, so a raised source reaches nothing; the leaf opens the mask for the eight FIFO and protocol sources and requires the output to follow them. `AVS_SLAVE_ISSUED_INTERRUPT` stays masked: the slave-interrupt detector re-arms it from the idle level of the AVS serial data line, so it returns immediately after each clear and would hold the output high regardless of the sources under test. The entry state is established by clearing every source and reading the output low, not assumed.

*Test Procedure*:

Drive `smc_avsbus_interrupt_sources_test_seq`. Each checker below is a named compare the DUT can fail; a token is emitted only after that compare holds.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AVS-CMD-FIFO-FULL-INT` |A full command FIFO reported `AVS_NORMAL_STATUS.CMD_FIFO_FULL` and raised `CMD_FIFO_FULL_INT`; once the clock was restored and the FIFO drained, `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` removed the flag
