|`CHK-AVS-CMD-FIFO-OVERFLOW-INT` |A further `AVS_CMD` write against the full FIFO was refused on the bus and dropped, leaving the occupancy unchanged, and raised `CMD_FIFO_OVERFLOW_INT`, which `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` removed
|`CHK-AVS-READBACK-FIFO-FULL-INT` |Responses filled the readback FIFO with `AVS_NORMAL_STATUS.READBACK_FIFO_FULL` set and raised `READBACK_FIFO_FULL_INT`; once the entries were read out, `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` removed the flag
|`CHK-AVS-READBACK-HAS-DATA-INT` |Responses waiting in the readback FIFO raised `READBACK_HAS_DATA_INT`; once it was emptied, `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` removed the flag
|`CHK-AVS-READBACK-UNDERFLOW-INT` |Reading `AVS_READBACK` on an empty FIFO raised `READBACK_UNDERFLOW_INT`, which `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` removed
|`CHK-AVS-INTERRUPT-CLEAR-SOURCES` |All five sources above were each raised, observed in `AVS_INTERRUPT`, cleared through the write-only `AVS_INTERRUPT_CLEAR` and observed gone
|`CHK-AVS-IRQ-UNMASKED` |With the FIFO sources unmasked the block's interrupt output was low at entry, rose as each source was raised and returned low once every one had been cleared
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all seven checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-231-smc_avsbus_clock_divider_test]]
===== 231 `smc_avsbus_clock_divider_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_avsbus_clock_divider_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); observation on `tb_avs_clk_from_dut`, the AVS clock at the pad

*Objective*: A written AVSBus clock divider setting must reach the clock the block drives.

*Test Coverage*:

* `AVS_CFG_1.AVS_CLOCK_SELECT` resets to a divided source, so the divider is already in the AVS clock path. Every other leaf leaves `CLK_DIVIDER_VALUE` and `CLK_DIVIDER_DUTY_CYCLE_NUMERATOR` at their reset, and the divider only reloads when a written setting differs from the one it holds, so nothing shows that a written setting reaches the clock at all.
* The divisor and the numerator are compared separately, so each is changed on its own: two divisors, each double the last, then a numerator change at a held divisor.
* The minimum the RDL states for the divisor. Both fields reset to 0, which is below it, and the RDL explains the reset as a value chosen to match the divider's own reset copy so that no reload happens. Writing 0 deliberately is therefore not the same as leaving it: the divider takes the minimum of 2. The leaf measures that, and restores the clock by writing the hardware defaults the RDL names rather than the register's reset value.
* Expected periods come from the reset measurement scaled by the programmed divisors, and the expected high fraction from the numerator over 256 as the RDL defines it; no rate is taken from the design.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Require `AVS_CLOCK_SELECT` to be a divided source and `STOP_AVS_CLOCK_ON_IDLE` to be clear, then measure the period and high fraction at the reset setting.
. Write each divisor with the premux clocks gated around the change, as the register documentation prescribes, and measure again.
. Change only the numerator and measure the high fraction and the period.
. Write a divisor of 0 and measure the minimum the divider assumes.
. Write the hardware defaults back and measure the reset rate again.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AVS-CLKDIV-PERIOD` |Each written divisor scaled the measured AVS clock period against the reset measurement
|`CHK-AVS-CLKDIV-DUTY` |A numerator change on its own moved the high fraction to what the numerator asks for, at an unchanged period
|`CHK-AVS-CLKDIV-MINIMUM` |A written divisor of 0 was taken as the minimum of 2, not as the reset behaviour
|`CHK-AVS-CLKDIV-RESTORE` |The hardware-default divisor and numerator returned the clock to its reset period and high fraction
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all four checkers hold after their compares. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-232-smc_i2c_target_addr_stretch_test]]
===== 232 `smc_i2c_target_addr_stretch_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_addr_stretch_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the `tb_i2c0_*` pads, which `+smc_i2c_shared_bus` puts in front of all three instances; observation on the resolved SCL net and on `ACQDATA`

*Objective*: A repeated START issued while the target's acquisition FIFO is full must hold the bus through the address phase until software drains it, on every instance.

*Test Coverage*:

* The address phase, which `smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test` does not reach: that leaf fills the FIFO part-way through a transfer, so the target stretches at a data byte. Holding the address phase needs the FIFO to still be out of room when a new address arrives, which only a repeated START can arrange -- a STOP would end the transfer and let software drain at leisure.
* Both settings of `CTRL.NACK_ADDR_AFTER_TIMEOUT`, which `i2c.rdl` gives as ACK the address byte even if a stretch timeout occurs (`0`, for SMBus) or NACK it (`1`). The two settings take the target down different paths to the same hold, and in both the transfer has to complete once the FIFO is drained.
* A third leg where the repeated START asks the target to *transmit*. The target leaves the address-phase stretch into the transmit stretch rather than into byte acquisition, and stays there until software supplies a byte: the drain alone does not release it, and the leg requires that.
* All three instances. Only I2C0 has pads of its own on the bench bus, so the other two are reached through `+smc_i2c_shared_bus` with only the instance under test enabled.
* No stretch timeout is enabled in either leg, so a drain by software is the only thing that can release the hold. A leg that passed without the drain would be a different mechanism.
* `CTRL.ACQ_START_STOP_EN` is set, so the acquired stream carries its own framing and the repeated START arrives as an `ACQDATA.SIGNAL` of `RESTART` after every byte written before it. The STOP entry is matched on its signal alone, since the RDL defines no byte for it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a target and require its acquisition FIFO to read empty and not full.
. Write bytes one at a time, checking `STATUS.ACQFULL` between them, and stop as soon as the target reports it is out of room.
. Issue a repeated START and the address again in the background, and measure the hold: only samples where the bus is low while the bench has released its own pull are counted.
. Require the bench's transfer to still be in flight, then drain `ACQDATA` until it completes.
. Compare the whole acquired stream against the bench's traffic and require every ACK bit to be low.
. Repeat with `CTRL.NACK_ADDR_AFTER_TIMEOUT` set, and again with the repeated START asking for a read, where the release needs a byte in `TXDATA` as well as the drain.
. Repeat the whole sequence on each instance in turn, with the other two disabled.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ADDR-STRETCH` |With `NACK_ADDR_AFTER_TIMEOUT` clear, the repeated START was held on the bus, the drain released it, and the acquired stream carried the restart entry after every earlier byte and ended in a STOP
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ADDR-STRETCH-NACK-MODE` |The same with `NACK_ADDR_AFTER_TIMEOUT` set, which takes the target's other address-phase path to the same result
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ADDR-STRETCH-READ` |When the held address phase belonged to a read, the target went on holding for a byte to send and was released only by a drain and a write into `TXDATA`, after which the bench read that byte back
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares, on every instance, and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-233-smc_i2c_target_ack_ctrl_test]]
===== 233 `smc_i2c_target_ack_ctrl_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_ack_ctrl_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the `tb_i2c0_*` pads; observation on the ACK bits the bench reads back and on `ACQDATA`

*Objective*: Software must be able to accept or refuse bytes through `TARGET_ACK_CTRL` while the target holds the bus for them.

*Test Coverage*:

* Both answers the register documentation gives software when `CTRL.ACK_CTRL_EN = 1` and the target stretches: reload `TARGET_ACK_CTRL.NBYTES` to accept, or write `TARGET_ACK_CTRL.NACK` to reject. Neither had been given to the block.
* The hold is the ACK counter's, not the acquisition FIFO's: `STATUS.ACQFULL` is required to be clear while `STATUS.ACK_CTRL_STRETCH` is set, so this cannot be read as the FIFO-full stretch that `smc_i2c_target_acq_stretch_test` covers.
* The count decrements per byte. `NBYTES` is reloaded with fewer bytes than remain, so the target has to stop again after exactly that many, and the leg requires exactly that number of holds -- no more, and none after the payload is covered.
* The write enable. `i2c_core.sv` gates `TARGET_ACK_CTRL.NBYTES` writes on the same stretch condition, so a reload that reads back as 0 means the gate was shut when the documentation says it is open.
* The rejected byte is identifiable: the acquisition FIFO must carry a NACKed-data entry, not just a NACK on the wire.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a target with `CTRL.ACK_CTRL_EN` and `CTRL.ACQ_START_STOP_EN`, and require `ACK_CTRL_STRETCH` clear before any transfer.
. Drive a multi-byte write from the bench in the background. `NBYTES` resets to 0, so the target holds at the first data byte.
. For each chunk: wait for `ACK_CTRL_STRETCH`, require `ACQFULL` clear, reload `NBYTES`, read it back, and wait for the hold to clear.
. Require the rest of the transfer to run out with no further hold, then compare the acquired stream and every ACK bit.
. Repeat with a second transfer, writing `TARGET_ACK_CTRL.NACK` at the hold instead, and require the bench to see NACKs from that byte on.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ACK-CTRL-ACCEPT` |The target held once per reload of `NBYTES`, each reload released it for exactly that many bytes, and every byte was acknowledged and acquired in order
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-ACK-CTRL-NACK` |Writing `TARGET_ACK_CTRL.NACK` at the hold made the target refuse the transfer, which the bench saw as NACKs and software can attribute from the NACKed-data entry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-234-smc_avsbus_retry_suppressed_test]]
===== 234 `smc_avsbus_retry_suppressed_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_avsbus_retry_suppressed_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the AVSBus sdata pad held at its idle level by the bench, so every reply reports the target as not responding

*Objective*: A retry budget of 0 must answer an unanswered command without resending it.

*Test Coverage*:

* `AVS_CFG_0.MAX_RETRIES = 0`, which the design reads as programmed to suppress retries: the failing reply is pushed to the readback FIFO as it stands and the controller carries on. `smc_avsbus_retry_exhaust_test` covers the other side of the same register with a budget to spend; the two leaves differ only in the budget.
* The absences that define suppression -- no retry state on the FSM debug bus, `TOTAL_RETRIES` unmoved, `AVS_MASTER_IS_RETRYING` never set, `MAX_RETRIES_ATTEMPTED_INT` never raised -- together with the one presence that separates suppression from dropping the reply: the readback FIFO still holds it.
* A positive control on the same registers and the same probes. The same commands are then given a budget of 1, and the counter and the debug bus have to move; without it the absences above could all be dead probes.
* The retry failure sources are cleared and required to stay clear only before the first command. Once the bench has held sdata idle through a burst, the controller's own resync traffic meets the same failing reply, so a later clear cannot be required to hold.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Hold the sdata pad at its idle level, save `AVS_CFG_0` and program `MAX_RETRIES` to 0.
. Clear both retry failure sources and require them clear, then read the retry counter.
. Queue two read commands and poll until the target is reported unresponsive, sampling the retrying flag on every poll.
. Require the counter, the flag, the debug bus and the budget-exhausted interrupt to show no retry, and the readback FIFO to hold the replies.
. Reprogram the budget to 1, queue the same commands, and require the counter and the debug bus to move.
. Restore `AVS_CFG_0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AVS-RETRY-SUPPRESSED` |With `MAX_RETRIES` at 0 the unanswered commands raised `SLAVE_UNRESPONSIVE_INT`, no retry was attempted by any of the four measures, and the failing replies still reached the readback FIFO
|`CHK-AVS-RETRY-SUPPRESSED-CONTROL` |The same commands with a budget of 1 moved `TOTAL_RETRIES` and put the retry states on the debug bus, so the suppressed leg is measuring live probes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-235-smc_i2c_target_nack_timeout_test]]
===== 235 `smc_i2c_target_nack_timeout_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_nack_timeout_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the `tb_i2c0_*` pads; observation on `TARGET_NACK_COUNT`, `ACQDATA` and the bus

*Objective*: A stretch nobody releases must end in the target's own NACK, in both directions, on every instance.

*Test Coverage*:

* The expiry of `TARGET_TIMEOUT_CTRL`, the cumulative limit on how long the target may stretch within one transaction. Every other stretch leaf is released by software first, so the limit had never been reached.
* Both consequences the RDL names for it -- NACK an incoming data byte, release SDA for an outgoing one -- one leg each.
* ACK Control Mode is the way into the incoming hold, so the target stops at the first data byte instead of after a whole acquisition FIFO of them. It is the same hold `smc_i2c_target_ack_ctrl_test` answers; the difference is that nothing answers it here.
* An empty transmit FIFO is the way into the outgoing hold, and a released SDA line reads as all ones at the bench.
* `TARGET_NACK_COUNT` is the register witness. `i2c_core.sv` increments it on the edge where the target decides to NACK the transaction, and the field is read-to-clear, so each leg reads it clear beforehand and requires exactly one afterwards -- not merely non-zero.
* The acquisition stream must end with the NACK-stop entry, so software can see the transaction ended badly rather than inferring it from the missing data.
* All three instances, reached through `+smc_i2c_shared_bus` with only the instance under test enabled.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a target with a short `TARGET_TIMEOUT_CTRL` allowance, and read `TARGET_NACK_COUNT` twice to clear it and prove it cleared.
. Incoming: with ACK Control Mode enabled and its count at reset, drive a write and let the hold run out.
. Require a NACK on the wire, exactly one counted transaction, and a NACK-stop entry closing the stream.
. Outgoing: with the transmit FIFO empty, drive a read and let the hold run out.
. Require all ones at the bench, exactly one counted transaction, and a NACK-stop entry.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-NACK-TIMEOUT-RX` |The allowance ran out at a held incoming byte: the byte was NACKed, the transaction counted once, and the stream closed with a NACK-stop entry
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-NACK-TIMEOUT-TX` |The allowance ran out with nothing to transmit: SDA was released so the bench read all ones, the transaction counted once, and the stream closed with a NACK-stop entry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares on every instance, and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-236-smc_i2c_target_fast_controller_test]]
===== 236 `smc_i2c_target_fast_controller_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_target_fast_controller_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cMasterVip` bit-banging the `tb_i2c0_*` pads, with its bit rate changed part-way through a transfer; observation on the ACK bits, `TARGET_NACK_COUNT` and `ACQDATA`

*Objective*: A controller that releases SCL before the target's data hold time has elapsed must have its transaction abandoned, on every instance.

*Test Coverage*:

* `TIMING3.THD_DAT`, the hold the target keeps with SCL low before driving an acknowledge. The threshold is a relation between that hold and the controller's SCL low period, so the leaf sets both: the target is given a hold far longer than any other leaf uses, and the bench is run at one rate well over it and one well under it.
* Both places the target makes the decision: the acknowledge of the address byte and the acknowledge of a data byte.
* The rate changes after the START in both fast legs, not before. The same hold governs both acknowledges, so at a single rate the address is always the first to go; and the bus monitor takes the same `THD_DAT` as its threshold for detecting a START (`i2c_bus_monitor.sv`), so a START clocked fast enough to violate the hold is not seen as a START at all. The measured consequence of getting that wrong was a transaction the target never joined: no acknowledge, no count, nothing acquired.
* The hold is also kept below half the slow rate's low period, so the target always finishes driving an acknowledge before the clock rises again and the control transfers are clean.
* A transfer entirely at the slow rate runs before and after the two fast legs, so the abandons are the difference the rate makes rather than a target that never works.
* All three instances, reached through `+smc_i2c_shared_bus` with only the instance under test enabled.
* `TARGET_NACK_COUNT` is the register witness. It takes the edge on which the target decides to NACK the transaction and is read-to-clear, so each leg clears it and requires exactly its own -- one for each abandon, none for either control.
* What reaches the acquisition FIFO separates the two abandons: the address leg records nothing for the address and closes with the NACK-stop entry, the data leg keeps its start entry but records no data.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a target with a long `TIMING3.THD_DAT`, read back the timing, and require the acquisition FIFO empty.
. Slow transfer: START, address, one data byte, STOP, all at the slow rate.
. Address leg: START at the slow rate, change rate, then the address byte.
. Data leg: START and address at the slow rate, park until the target's hold on that acknowledge has finished, change rate, then the data byte.
. Slow transfer again.
. For each leg, drain the acquisition FIFO once its level has settled and read `TARGET_NACK_COUNT`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-FAST-CTRL-CONTROL` |Both slow transfers were acknowledged throughout, acquired in full and counted no NACK
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-FAST-CTRL-ADDR` |The address clocked under the hold time got no acknowledge, was counted once, and left only the NACK-stop entry
|`CHK-I2C-TGT-FAST-CTRL-DATA` |With the address acknowledged at the slow rate, the data byte clocked under the hold time got no acknowledge, was counted once, and left no data entry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-237-smc_i2c_controller_stretch_test]]
===== 237 `smc_i2c_controller_stretch_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_controller_stretch_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cEepromSlave` on the `tb_i2c0_*` pads as the target, a second bench pad driver as the device that holds the clock; observation on the transfer's own length and on what the target received

*Objective*: The DUT's I2C controller must wait out a held clock and still deliver the transfer.

*Test Coverage*:

* The controller's wait for SCL to rise, which no leaf had taken: every controller leaf in the package talks to a device that never holds the clock.
* Six hold points across one transfer, so the wait is taken from the address byte, its acknowledge and the payload bytes rather than from one place in the bit loop.
* The hold is a second driver on the open-drain bus, so the controller's own release cannot lift the line -- the same thing a stretching target does.
* No timeout is enabled. A controller that gave up instead of waiting would show as a byte the target never received, not as a timeout the leaf could read as success, and `CONTROLLER_EVENTS` is required to stay clear on every leg.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a host with the bench EEPROM target on the pads.
. Queue a three-entry write and time it with nothing holding the line.
. For each hold point: queue the same write to a fresh offset, hold SCL low for a fixed span starting that far into the transfer, then release.
. Require each held transfer to run at least three quarters of the hold longer than the clean one, to deliver the queued bytes, and to leave `CONTROLLER_EVENTS` clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-STRETCH-CLEAN` |The unheld transfer's length, which the held ones are measured against
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-STRETCH-HELD` |At every hold point the transfer ran longer by about the hold and still delivered every byte
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-238-smc_i2c_controller_control_symbol_test]]
===== 238 `smc_i2c_controller_control_symbol_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_controller_control_symbol_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cEepromSlave` on the `tb_i2c0_*` pads as the target, a second bench pad driver as the device that makes the edge; observation on `INTR_STATE.SDA_UNSTABLE` and `STATUS.HOSTIDLE`

*Objective*: An SDA edge inside an SCL high window is a START or a STOP, and the controller must report it and drop the transfer.

*Test Coverage*:

* The control-symbol check the controller makes in every state where it holds SCL released. `smc_i2c_controller_sda_interference_test` pulls SDA low at time offsets measured in bit periods, which lands the edge wherever it falls -- with this controller's timing usually while SCL is low, where it is a different event. This leaf counts SCL rising edges from the START on the bus and pulls inside the high window of a chosen one, so the edge is a control symbol by construction and lands in a chosen slot.
* The two slots where the line is high because it is released: the acknowledge of an address nobody answers, and a one bit of a byte the target is returning.
* The pull is released inside the same high window, because one that outlasts the slot reaches a state where the controller drives the line and is interference there instead.
* The slot numbering is anchored on the START seen on the pads, not on a status read: a poll returns some cycles after the controller leaves idle, by which time bits have already been clocked, which a first version of this leaf measured as a slot two positions out.

*Known limitation*: the other two slots where the controller holds SCL released -- a bit it is transmitting, and the not-acknowledge it drives after a read -- are high only because the controller is driving them. An external pull there is interference, which the design detects from the level alone and acts on a cycle before the control-symbol check, which needs two consecutive samples. Both are left uncovered rather than claimed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a host with the bench EEPROM target on the pads, and run one uninterrupted write.
. For each slot: re-enable the controller from scratch, arm the injector, queue the transfer, wait for the START on the bus, count SCL rises and pull SDA low inside the chosen one.
. Require `INTR_STATE.SDA_UNSTABLE` and a return to idle.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-SYMBOL-CLEAN` |An uninterrupted write reached the target and left `SDA_UNSTABLE` clear
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-SYMBOL-UNSTABLE` |An edge made inside a chosen SCL high window raised `SDA_UNSTABLE` and returned the controller to idle, at both slots
|===

*Pass Criteria*: both checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Nothing is asserted about the transfer after an injection: on this DUT the next transaction from the same controller is NACKed even once the events are cleared, so each leg re-enables the controller from scratch. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-239-smc_uart_rbr_error_path_test]]
===== 239 `smc_uart_rbr_error_path_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_uart_rbr_error_path_test`

*BFM / Model*: UART3's transmit pin crossed to UART0's receive pin (`+smc_uart_cross_3to0`); observation on `LSR` and `IIR`

*Objective*: A character that arrives broken while the receive FIFO is off must carry its flags through the receiver buffer register.

*Test Coverage*:

* The second of the two receive paths `uart_core.sv` selects with `FCR.FIFO_ENABLE`. Each path carries its own copy of the error flags. `smc_uart_error_conditions_test` breaks characters on the FIFO path and `smc_uart_core_mode_select_test` sends clean ones through the register path, so a broken character had never taken it.
* The two kinds two UARTs can disagree into existence: a parity disagreement, and a word length disagreement that makes the receiver sample its stop bit where a data bit still is.
* The framing character carries a one, so the frame is not the all-zero one a break is, and the leg requires `LSR.BI` to stay clear. A break sets the framing flag as well, so a break alone cannot separate the two.
* The receive trigger level, which `uart_core.sv` builds from `ECR.RCVR_TRIGGER_MS2B` and `FCR.RCVR_TRIGGER` together and compares against the depth the FIFO was built with. Every other leaf programs a level the design supports; this one programs a level above the depth, where the watermark can never be reached.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With both UARTs' FIFOs disabled and the two ends agreeing, send one character and require no error flag.
. Set the transmitter to odd parity and the receiver to even, send one character and require `LSR.PE`.
. Set the transmitter to eight bits and the receiver to five, send a character with a zero at the receiver's stop position and a one before it, and require `LSR.FE` without `LSR.BI`.
. Program the trigger level above the FIFO depth with the FIFOs on, send one character, and require `LSR.DR` with no received-data-ready interrupt.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-UART-RBR-CLEAN` |A character both ends agreed on reached the receiver buffer register with no error flag
|`CHK-UART-RBR-ERRORS` |A parity disagreement and a word length disagreement each reached the receiver buffer register with their own flag and no other
|`CHK-UART-RXILVL-UNSUPPORTED` |With the trigger level above the FIFO depth a character still arrived and the received-data-ready interrupt stayed away
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `uart`.

'''''

[[sec-240-smc_i2c_controller_exits_test]]
===== 240 `smc_i2c_controller_exits_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_i2c_controller_exits_test`

*BFM / Model*: `SmcI2cEepromSlave` on the `tb_i2c0_*` pads for the first leg; an address no device answers for the second

*Objective*: Two endings of a controller transaction no leaf had produced, and `CONTROLLER_EVENTS` as a register rather than a flag.

*Test Coverage*:

* A read whose last byte carries no stop. The controller returns to the format FIFO for the entries queued behind it instead of stopping, which is the only way it leaves the read acknowledge into `PopFmtFifo`. The target has to see a single stop for the whole sequence, not one per entry.
* A NACK left unhandled. `i2c.rdl` describes `HOST_NACK_HANDLER_TIMEOUT` as the limit on how long software may leave the controller halted on an unexpected NACK; past it `CONTROLLER_EVENTS.UNHANDLED_NACK_TIMEOUT` is raised beside the NACK. Every other leaf clears a NACK as soon as it sees one.
* `CONTROLLER_EVENTS` clears on a written one, so two writes that must *not* clear it are made first: a word of zeros over a set bit, and a byte-sized write to the far end of the register, which leaves the lane carrying the bit disabled. The bit is read back set after each, and only a written one clears it. Both set fields are checked this way.

*Known limitation*: the controller's third ending, the automatic stop it makes when `CTRL.ENABLEHOST` is cleared with a transaction still open, is not driven. `i2c_controller_fsm.sv:291` clears `trans_started` in the same cycle the enable drops, so the `trans_started && !host_enable_i` arms in `Idle` and in `PopFmtFifo` are each one cycle wide. With the controller parked mid-transaction -- every entry consumed, no stop given, `STATUS.HOSTIDLE` clear -- clearing the enable returned it to idle without the bench target seeing a stop.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up I2C0 as a host with the bench EEPROM target on the pads.
. Queue a write of an offset, a repeated start and a one-byte read *without* a stop, then a further write with a stop. Require the byte read back, the later write at the target, and one stop.
. Arm `HOST_NACK_HANDLER_TIMEOUT`, queue a write to an address no device answers, and wait for `CONTROLLER_EVENTS.NACK` and then `UNHANDLED_NACK_TIMEOUT`.
. For each set field: write zeros, read it back set; write a byte to the far end of the register, read it back set; write a one, read it back clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-READ-CONTINUES` |A read whose last byte carried no stop returned to the format FIFO, the entry behind it reached the target, and the target saw a single stop
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-NACK-TIMEOUT` |A NACK left unhandled past the programmed limit raised `UNHANDLED_NACK_TIMEOUT` beside it
|`CHK-I2C-CTRL-EVENTS-RETAIN` |Both set fields survived a word of zeros and a byte write that left their lane disabled, and cleared only on a written one
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`, `i2c`.

'''''

[[sec-241-smc_avsbus_frame_sequencing_test]]
===== 241 `smc_avsbus_frame_sequencing_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_avsbus_frame_sequencing_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); the AVSBus sdata pad driven at its idle or responding level by the bench

*Objective*: How one AVSBus frame follows another, which the command mix decides.

*Test Coverage*:

* A command queued on its own. `architecture.adoc` describes a frame as a first subframe, as many middle subframes as there are further commands ready, and a last one, so with nothing behind it the first subframe is followed straight by the last. Every other leaf queues its commands in one burst, so that path had never been taken.
* A command queued while the bus is already running, spaced so that one of them arrives during a last subframe and starts a new frame from the end of it instead of letting the bus go idle.
* A reply the master had to buffer. A frame retried while a later frame is already on the wire leaves that later reply pending; when the master returns to it, it either retries that one too or, if it is good, resynchronises the slave. The bench decides which by driving the pad: the retries are provoked at the idle level and the pad is then released to its responding level, so the buffered reply is a good one.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With the pad at its responding level and the readback FIFO drained, queue one command and require a response and the last-subframe shift state on the debug bus.
. Queue several commands one at a time with a short gap, and require a response for each.
. With a retry budget programmed and the pad at its idle level, queue two commands and wait for `TOTAL_RETRIES` to move; release the pad to its responding level and require the slave-resynchronisation state on the debug bus.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AVS-SINGLE-COMMAND` |A command with nothing behind it produced its response and took the machine through the last-subframe shift rather than a middle one
|`CHK-AVS-LATE-COMMAND` |Commands written one at a time while the bus ran all produced responses
|`CHK-AVS-BUFFERED-REPLY` |After a retry with a second frame on the wire, a buffered reply that turned out good took the master to the slave resynchronisation rather than another retry
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all three checkers hold after their compares and every sequence assert holds. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[smc-formal-plan]]
=== Formal Verification Plan

Formal property checking is not part of the SMC DV package at this
revision. Every row of this plan is credited by simulation alone, and
`hw/sys/smc/dv/` carries no formal collateral: no `smc_formal_cfg.toml`
beside `smc_sim_cfg.toml`, no `testlists/formal.toml`, and no `formal/`
directory holding a property set, a bind file, a task file or a reset
environment. `python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --validate-configs` lists no
`smc (formal)` view, `--list` shows no `fv` row for `smc`, and
`python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut smc --mode formal` stops with
`formal config not found`. Hosted CI runs no formal job for any DUT
(`tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`, Formal verification), so the PR gate and the
nightly and weekly tiers under Regression Groups are complete without one.
The absence is a scope decision of this package, not a deferred item: no
Known Limitations row records it.

A formal view for `smc` is added the way `hw/sys/dtp/dv/` adds one, the
runner's reference implementation: an `smc_formal_cfg.toml` beside the sim
config on the `native-formal` profile -- or a `formal_cfg` pointer in the
registry or the site layer -- naming the formal top and the Bender targets
of the design closure, a `[testlist]` of formal items and one
`[formal.apps.<app>.sby]` table per app; the items in `testlists/formal.toml`;
the properties, the bind file, the task file and the reset environment under
`formal/`, in the style of `hw/common/dv/docs/formal-property-style.adoc`; a
`## Formal` section in `README.md` in the shape that chapter gives; and this
section rewritten in the shape of the one in
`hw/sys/dtp/dv/docs/DTP_VPLAN.adoc` -- scope, properties with the plan rows
they corroborate, tasks and depths, running and grading -- with the FAIL path
checked by inverting one assertion. `--validate-configs` then lists
`smc (formal)` and this section stops describing an absence.

=== Regression Groups

[cols="1,3",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Meaning
|`smoke` |CI gate on every pull request and push (`sim.yml`): three toolchain-free bring-up leaves
|`all` |Every test this plan grades and the coverage set: `hosted` + `fw` + `dual`, no overlap, nothing left over; `expected_count` is the membership gate
|`hosted` |Toolchain-free class of `all`, single-instance model; the nightly and weekly tiers on the hosted runners, three seeds per leaf
|`fw` |Firmware class of `all`: the fourteen CPU-boot leaves whose image the `c_compile` stage builds with a picolibc-enabled RISC-V GCC or the toolchain container
|`dual` |SMC_DUAL class of `all`: the three `target = "dual"` leaves, each loading a ROM or firmware image
|`axil`, `clock`, `combined`, `cpu`, `gpio`, `i2c`, `irq`, `reset`, `uart` |Feature subsets of `all` for a local run of one area; each lives in its feature testlist
|`occp_boot`, `occp_dual` |Runtime hold-outs run on demand: the production ROM to its OCCP loop, and the dual-SMC unsecure boot. Not in `all`
|`held_out` |Leaves waiting on an RTL fix or ruling, run on demand to re-measure. Not in `all`
|===

`README.md` owns the commands that run these groups (CI, nightly, hosted and
single-test forms). Every leaf outside `all` is defined in
`testlists/holdout.toml` and has its card under <<smc-held-out-testcases>>.


=== Signoff Package

The SMC signoff package is this book plus the tracker: the plans, contracts,
dispositions and scope exclusions below hold across candidates, and the
tracker's signoff item carries what belongs to one candidate (the commit, the
run trees it was qualified with, the open issues and their release dispositions).
Feature credit is the <<smc-scope-traceability-candidate,Scope and Traceability>> record above.

=== Release Qualification

A release candidate is qualified by regression evidence on the supported
framework and simulator pairs, graded by the runner's leaf rules and retained
with the run tree; a passing set of individual tests is not a qualification.
The runner's grading, record and CI contracts are in `tools/dv/doc/run-dv.adoc`.

[[smc-release-matrix]]
==== SMC release regression matrix

This chapter defines the mandatory qualification set: the *live* CI matrix on
Verilator — `smoke` per PR, `hosted` nightly and weekly. It is not narrowed
to a smaller tier. The leaves no tier
runs, and why, are recorded in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>.

`--dut smc` already instantiates `smc_wrapper`. There is no separate
`--dut smc_wrapper` job.

[[smc-release-combinations]]
===== Mandatory combinations

[cols="2,2,1,3",options="header",]
|===
|Tier |Command |Seeds |Blocks
|PR |`--dut smc --tool verilator --items smoke` |1 |PR merge (`sim.yml`)
|Nightly |`--dut smc --tool verilator --items hosted` |3 |Release qualification (`regress.yml`)
|Weekly |`--dut smc --tool verilator --items hosted` |3 |Coverage / dashboard; same group
|===

Group sizes are the `expected_count` guards: `all` and `hosted` in
`hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/all.toml`.

[[smc-release-credit]]
===== Credit versus the CI group

`smoke` and `hosted` are debug breadth. Feature credit is the SCOPE YES rows,
not every SAMPLE observe name in the group. `smc_i3c_to_fabric_test` is
HCI_VERSION decode (`proxy=True`) and is not in `smoke`.

The coverage gate is defined in `SMC_FCOV.adoc` (Coverage Policy); this chapter does
not absorb it.

=== Component Signoff

One subsection per signoff area. Each records the evidence contract its
scenarios meet, the negative-validation hooks that must fail a run, and the
disposition of every row whose realisation differs from its plan text.

the SCOPE matrix in <<smc-scope-traceability-candidate,Scope and Traceability>>. Live membership is
`hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/all.toml`. Launch is `--dut smc` (the TB instantiates
`smc_wrapper`); there is no `--dut smc_wrapper`.

`allow_timeout=True` stays a reviewed exception: helpers in
`smc_csr_seq_utils.py` that set it score the timeout, and each live
call site keeps a rationale comment (README gate). Force / placeholder
paths receive no credit.

Depth groups `reset` / `clock` / `irq` remain debug breadth. They are
not an extra feature-credit set.

On Verilator and Xcelium the `och_prim` `prim_sync2` / `prim_sync3` cells are
replaced by the behavioural stand-ins in `tb/verilator_stubs/`; VCS drops them.
Every leaf of
`reset.toml`, `clock.toml` and `irq.toml` has a row in the *Bench stand-ins*
section of <<smc-deferred-disposition>> stating which crossing sits on its
proof path, what its verdict reads, and the result of a VCS run on the product
cells. Two runs supply those results. The group control run (`--items reset
clock irq`) covered the 35 leaves of the `reset`, `clock` and `irq` groups: 34
entries of the three files plus `smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test`, an
`irq` group member defined in `combined.toml`. The five entries the three
files hold outside those groups -- `smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test`,
`smc_flr_sanity_test`, `smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test`,
`smc_zeroer_dma_timeout_test` and `smc_mailbox_irq_test` -- were not in it;
the separate run `20260914_031352__vcs__standin_files_minus_groups`, with the waiver
below in place, grades them 5 of 5 PASS on both the cocotb verdict and the
runner, with no assertion firing after time 0. Two findings of the group
control run belong to this signoff. The first
is closed: the VCS flow graded every leaf FAIL on a time-0 PeakRDL external-ack
assertion class (`assert_bad_ext_wr_ack` / `assert_bad_ext_rd_ack` in
`cpu_ctrl_reg`, `reset_unit_reg` and `system_timer_octs_reg`) that fires once
before reset, while the ack strobes are still X, and that the cocotb verdicts
do not depend on. That class is waived at time 0 only, in `smc_sim_cfg.toml`
`[pass_fail]` in the form SEP uses for `abr_reg`, and the waived re-run
`20260914_035111__vcs__reset` grades 35 of 35 PASS. The second stays open:
`smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test` fires the product assertion
`CheckMutualExclusionClk1` in `avsbus_controller.u_refclk_apbclk_mux`
(`prim_ag_clk_mux.sv`) at 4688 ns on seed 1611842931. The waiver does not
cover it -- a replay of the leaf on that seed grades FAIL on that line -- and
no Verilator run can observe it because assertions are compiled out there.
That firing remains an open item of this signoff; the condition that closes
it, and the tracker for it, are the *Closes when* cell of the `prim_sync2/3`
control row in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>.

The cool-reset OUT names (`smc_cool_reset_from_primary_chiplet_test`,
`smc_cool_reset_from_bmc_test`, `smc_cool_reset_x_cool_reset_test`) have rows
in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>.

=== Known Limitations

[cols=",,",options="header",]
|===
|Limitation |Impact |Resolution Path
|The SEP_IN local fabric folds every request into the `LOCAL_BASE`-aligned, `REGION_SIZE`-byte alias aperture (`SMC_BASE_CONFIG.REGION_SIZE`, reset `0x0100_0000`, so `addr[23:0]` survive and `LOCAL_BASE` is prefixed above them), so the cluster-local window `0xC800_xxxx` answers from `0xC000_xxxx` |No access from this testbench reaches a Bus Error Unit; `smc_cluster_beu_test` locks the address fold instead |By design: the SEP and SMU fabric decodes prevent such an address reaching the port. BEU behaviour needs a path that bypasses the mask
|Verilator is a 2-state simulator |Resolvability predicates (`is_resolvable`) cannot fail on a Verilator run, so X-propagation claims are not closed there |Retain a VCS or Xcelium run for the scenarios whose evidence depends on 4-state behaviour
|PLL and PVT are placeholder RTL in this harness |The PLL/PVT scenarios in the wider plan cannot be run: there is no design behind the register interface |Needs the adopter's PLL/PVT blocks integrated
|The I3C DAT/DCT RAM is not present in `tb_top` |CCC/IBI scenarios that need device-address-table state cannot run; `smc_i3c_to_fabric_test` is decode-only |Add a DAT/DCT model, or drive the sequences from a full I3C host model
|The DTP CSR window has no responder |AXI-Lite accesses to it would wedge, so the boundary is left idle rather than answered by a placeholder |Connect the DTP CSR subordinate; SMU already connects the same port
|The cluster PLIC aperture (0xC400_0000) is not writable from SEP_IN AXI |No *cocotb* testcase can cover the PLIC register file from the external port: PRIORITY[1] accepts a write with OKAY and stores nothing, and the +2 MB context pages return non-OKAY. The measurement is recorded against `smc_cluster_plic_csr_test` in <<smc-deferred-disposition>>; the four register-level properties -- context independence across the eight threshold pages, source 0 and PENDING read-only, PENDING tracking a real interrupt -- need a write path. The PLIC's *function* is covered: `smc_fw_plic_claim_test` programs it from the CPU and claims through it |Expose a write path from SEP_IN, or extend the firmware image to read back what it programmed
|SV covergroups are not supported by Verilator |Functional coverage is Python-side on the open-source flow |See `SMC_FCOV.adoc`
|`smc_state_fault_inject_test` reaches the illegal eFuse and Zeroer state encodings only by forcing the state flops through the `tb_*_state_inject` hooks in `tb_top` |The stimulus is a testbench force, not a product-port access. The leaf runs in `all` and `hosted` and scores the DUT's reaction over the CSRs, so its PASS is evidence for the fail-closed behaviour and not for the reachability of an illegal encoding from any port |Reach an illegal encoding from a product port, or accept the force as the only stimulus for this property, recorded here
|Thirteen test modules written against this bench are out of the tree |Their blockers are outside the test: adopter placeholder PLL/PVT RTL, a wrapper pin that does not exist, an idle DTP boundary, a PLIC aperture SEP_IN cannot write, a CLINT window SEP_IN cannot reach, TB glue, or a pad-level VIP the bench lacks. The retired-module table in <<smc-deferred-disposition>> names the blocker per row |Clear the blocker named in the row; the module is restored from git history
|The raw OTP window has no responder on `smc_wrapper` |No testcase exercises a permission check at the raw OTP window -- the OTP bank itself, as distinct from the eFuse-shim CSR window `smc_efuse_chip_config_read_test_seq` reads over SEP_IN and the JTAG access-control path `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test` drives. `smc_efuse_permission_boundary_test` stays on the CSR side of the eFuse boundary and claims only the chip-config reads it makes. The permission semantics this bench can reach are covered elsewhere: `smc_efuse_jtag_lc_access_matrix_test` (lifecycle-gated JTAG-side access) and `smc_efuse_locked_access_interrupt_test` (locked-shadow access) |Add a raw-OTP responder to the bench, or assign the raw OTP permission path to the eFuse block-level TB
|`smc_octs_sanity_test` carries an OCTS name but drives the AVSBus status CSR path and the sideband observability probe |Its PASS is evidence for the AVS status window decode and the bench sideband probes, not for any OCTS register or pad; its protocol-VIP record is booked as a proxy. OCTS is covered by `smc_octs_dual_sync_test` (`dual`) and `smc_fw_octs_p0_primary_test` (`fw`) |Rename the leaf to what it drives, or give it OCTS stimulus and re-review the card
|`feat_ctrl` bit 37 `SMC_FUSE_TEST` is not consumed by SMC |The lifecycle-gated JTAG eFuse matrix cannot observe that bit; the connection stays a negative item |Cover the bit once SMC consumes it
|UART0 transmits two stop bits for a 5-bit word with `LCR.STB` set, where `uart_16550_main.rdl` describes 1.5 (#2121) |`smc_uart_baud_word_parity_format_test` measures the frame length on the TX pad for every format; for the two 5-bit `STB = 1` formats it admits either 1.5 or 2 stop bits, records which one was measured, and so does not claim the RDL count for them (one stop bit still fails). Every other format is held to the RDL count |Fix the transmitter or the RDL description under #2121, then drop the admitted alternative so the two formats are held to one count
|`INTR_STATUS.LOG_WRITE_ERR` has no firmware-reachable stimulus in this wrap |The log-write AXI-Lite master targets the wrapper's own `uart_16550` block, which ties write-error to 0 and answers every write OKAY. `smc_fw_uart_log_engine_boundary_backpressure_test` covers length and TX backpressure only; `INTR_TEST.LOG_WRITE_ERR` proves the interrupt plumbing, not error detection |A bench-side fault injector on the log-write AXI-Lite B channel
|===

[[smc-held-out-testcases]]
==== Held-out testcases

Every leaf defined in `testlists/holdout.toml`. None is a member of `all`;
each card below states what a run of the leaf must show and records no pass.

Filed-defect reproducers, when one exists, form the `rtl_issue` group: tests
whose job is to fail, each holding the runnable evidence for one RTL defect so
the defect has a measurement rather than a description alone. Such a leaf is
enrolled through `rtl_issue` in `testlists/holdout.toml` -- no `ci` tag, not in
`smoke`, `hosted` or `all` -- with `expect_fail` naming the issue and
`expect_fail_match` pinned to the assertion it raises; the weekly tier runs the
group on a `regress.yml` row of its own and grades the recorded failure as
PASS, so the row goes red (`expected_fail_passed`) the week a defect is fixed
and the leaf moves to its feature testlist. Each carries an arm leg that must
pass before its defect leg is scored, so a stuck-at field or a dead probe
cannot satisfy the defect leg for the wrong reason. The group has no member at
present: `smc_deadspace_decode_test` left it when #585 closed and runs from
`fabric.toml` in `all` and `hosted`.

[[sec-161-smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test]]
===== 161 `smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test`

*BFM / Model*: no model on either side -- `u_bfm` runs the `occp_unsecure_boot_test` DV ROM image against `u_dut` running the production boot ROM

*Objective*: OCCP unsecure boot end to end between two real SMCs over the shared I3C bus.

*Test Coverage*:

* Payload staging into the controller's scratch SRAM over its inbound AXI manager.
* The scratch 5-8 host protocol between testbench and controller firmware.
* ENTDAA, chunked OCCP `WRITE`s and the `JUMP`, firmware to firmware.
* Real traffic on the shared I3C bus, counted by the testbench rather than reported by firmware.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Hold the controller parked on its target-ready handshake so the staging window stays open.
. Draw the staging address and the transfer destination per run from the OCCP window, so honouring the WRITE address field and always writing from the bottom of the window are distinguishable.
. Stage the payload over AXI, seed scratch 5-8, and release both CPUs.
. Wait for the target ROM to reach its OCCP command loop, then let the controller drive the transfer and the JUMP.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OCCP-TARGET-READY` |Target ROM is in its OCCP command loop, by POST code and trace
|`CHK-OCCP-PRECONDITION` |Target scratch 0 does not already hold the pass value before the transfer
|`CHK-OCCP-FW-SEED` |Controller RNG seeded from `RANDOM_SEED` and read back
|`CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-STAGED` |The payload reads back from the controller's SRAM exactly as staged
|`CHK-OCCP-HOST-PROTOCOL` |Controller scratch 5-8 reads back exactly as seeded
|`CHK-OCCP-TRANSFER-LANDED` |Target SRAM at the drawn destination holds the transferred image -- first and last word, read through the `smc_scratch_map_pkg` backdoor that `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test` holds against AXI. Raises on a mismatch
|`CHK-OCCP-BUS-ACTIVITY` |At least one I3C channel saw both an SCL fall and a START condition during the transfer, counted by the testbench rather than reported by firmware. Requiring the START rules out a clocking artefact moving `scl_falls` without a framed transfer
|`CHK-OCCP-CONTROLLER-NO-FAIL` |Controller scratch 0 is not `TEST_FAIL`
|`CHK-OCCP-JUMP-EXECUTED` |Target retired PC lands inside the transferred image
|`CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-EXECUTED` |Target scratch 0 holds `TEST_PASS`, written by the payload
|===

*Pass Criteria*: all ten checkers hold after their compares -- `CHK-OCCP-TARGET-READY`, `CHK-OCCP-PRECONDITION`, `CHK-OCCP-FW-SEED`, `CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-STAGED`, `CHK-OCCP-HOST-PROTOCOL`, `CHK-OCCP-TRANSFER-LANDED`, `CHK-OCCP-BUS-ACTIVITY`, `CHK-OCCP-CONTROLLER-NO-FAIL`, `CHK-OCCP-JUMP-EXECUTED`, `CHK-OCCP-PAYLOAD-EXECUTED`. The payload destination is whatever `pick_payload_addresses` draws for that seed; the landing check compares first and last word at that drawn address.

`CHK-OCCP-TRANSFER-LANDED` depends on `smc_scratch_map_pkg` decoding the drawn destination the same way `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test` holds against AXI. `pick_payload_addresses` draws from anywhere in the OCCP window, so the peek is almost never reading offset 0, and a decode right only for the first 256 bytes of the window would look in the wrong bank. The six-offset agreement in `smc_dual_axi_sram_probe_test` is what makes the checker able to raise.

Held out of `all` on runtime (hours of wall clock per run). Enrolled in: `occp_dual` only -- held out of `all` on runtime and run on demand as `occp_dual` (see <<smc-held-out-testcases>>). This card records what a run must show, not a pass.

'''''



[[sec-162-smc_prod_rom_occp_ready_test]]
===== 162 `smc_prod_rom_occp_ready_test`

*Source Module*: `smc_prod_rom_occp_ready_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (AXI); production boot ROM built with `I3C_CORE=swap`

*Objective*: Production boot ROM reaches its OCCP command processing loop.

*Test Coverage*:

* The real ROM from reset through strap and fuse read, security checks, interface map and OCCP init.
* No bus traffic and no controller: this is the single-instance precondition the OCCP boot tests build on.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run with `+rom_bin64=prod_rom.bin64` and `+smc_hold_cpu_boot`, holding `boot_stall` from t=0.
. Program `RESET_VECTOR` before any core fetches, then release.
. Read the ROM's POST code from scratch 1 and require boot phase `[31:28]` to reach `OCCP_PROC`.
. Cross-check against CPU ROM fetch activity, so the phase cannot be credited to anything but the ROM executing.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PROD-ROM-OCCP-PROC` |POST code reaches the OCCP command loop, corroborated by ROM fetch activity
|===

*Pass Criteria*: the POST code reaches `OCCP_PROC` and ROM fetch activity corroborates it. Real fuse sense is used -- no `+skip_fuse_sense` -- so the ROM reads straps and fuses itself. Enrolled in: `occp_boot` only -- held out of `all` on runtime and run on demand as `occp_boot` (see <<smc-held-out-testcases>>). This card records what a run must show, not a pass.

'''''

`smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test` has an entry in `holdout.toml` and no card: it
fails the rate comparison it was written to make (`clk_rst.adoc` places the OCTS
system timer in the reference clock domain; `smc_peripherals.sv` clocks it from
`clk_smc_i`), and is re-measured on demand.

[[smc-deferred-disposition]]
==== SMC deferred and OUT disposition

This is the disposition of record for every test name the package catalogues
without a module under `hw/sys/smc/dv/cocotb/tests/`, and for every enrolled
leaf that no scheduled tier runs (<<smc-scheduled-holdouts>>). Nothing here is
enrolled PASS. Every `@pyuvm.test()` module under `cocotb/tests/` is in at
least one testlist (`smc_base_test` and `smc_dual_base_test` are base classes,
not tests); a catalogued name without a module is one of two kinds:

* A *retired test module* targets this bench and its blocker is
  outside the test: adopter placeholder RTL, a wrapper pin that does not
  exist, an idle boundary, an aperture or window SEP_IN cannot write or
  reach, TB glue, or a VIP the bench lacks. Its code, and
  the `seq_lib/<name>_seq.py` sequence where it had one, is in git history
  and is restored when the blocker named in its row clears.
* A *catalog-only name* never had a module here: a commercial alias whose
  intent a live enrolled name already proves, or a body that needs stimulus,
  RTL or a firmware image this bench does not have.

Buckets:

* `adopter-pending OUT` — waits on adopter IP, a product pin, firmware/ROM, or a TB terminator.
* `child-TB-owned` — protocol completeness belongs to a block-level TB (I3C, DTP).
* `waived` — fake BFM, TB-glue, or a commercial alias already covered by a live name.

[[smc-idle-boundaries]]
===== Idle smc_wrapper boundaries (OUT)

These ports have no TB terminator on `--dut smc` / `smc_wrapper`
(`hw/sys/smc/dv/models/README.md`). They are OUT rather than terminated by a
placeholder responder. Block-level I3C and DTP signoff stay on those TBs;
do not count a wrapper terminator as that proof.

[cols="2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Boundary |Disposition |Reintroduction trigger
|I3C DAT/DCT |adopter-pending OUT |Real DAT/DCT macros on `smc_wrapper` / `smu_wrapper`. Protocol CCC/IBI remains I3C block DV. Do not credit `smc_i3c_to_fabric_test` as this proof.
|DTP CSR AXI-Lite |adopter-pending OUT |A real DTP CSR slave on `smc_wrapper`, or accept SMU-level DTP tests as the only wrapper-adjacent surface. SMU already wires DTP internally.
|===

[[smc-bench-tie-offs]]
===== Bench tie-offs

A tie-off is a constant on an `smc_wrapper` input port in `tb_top.sv` (the
single-instance `u_dut` and the `smc_dual_inst` wrapper instance). Every
`--dut smc` / `smc_wrapper` and SMC_DUAL leaf runs under it, on every tool.
Each row states the behaviour the constant keeps out of reach, the enrolled
claims that would change if the pin were driven, and the control that closes
the row.

[cols="2,1,5,3,4,1,1",options="header"]
|===
|Tie-off |Value |Behaviour made unreachable |Tests whose claims would change |Control / closes when |Owner |Review date
|`sep_security_disable_i` (`tb_top.sv`, both `smc_wrapper` instances)
|`1'b0`
|The eFuse sense bypass (`hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Security Disable Bypass Mechanism"). On the product the pin is the SEP `security_disable_o` token-match result (`sep.sv`, wired through `smu.sv`); inside SMC it reaches only `smc_efuse_wrapper` -> `efuse_interface_controller.security_disable_i`. SMC builds the controller with `HAS_LC_STATE=0` (`smc_efuse_wrapper.sv`), so the bypass takes the non-LC branch: the controller drives `shadow_regs_o` from the shadow registers whenever fuse sensing has finished or the pin is high, and drives all zeros otherwise (`efuse_interface_controller.sv:449`); its command mux routes the shadow-register sense FSM to the bank only while sensing is not done and the pin is low (`efuse_interface_controller.sv:759`). With the pin at 1 the FSM's reads are never routed, so `fuse_sense_done` never rises, the shadow registers keep their all-zero reset value and are exposed unsensed, and `fuse_reset_n` -- and with it `rst_warm` (`smc_reset_ctrl.sv:135`) -- never release: the warm domain never leaves reset. With the pin at 0 every run senses the bank, which is what the product does on a chip whose SEC_DIS token did not match.
|None of the enrolled claims: no leaf claims the bypass, and every eFuse leaf proves the sensed path, which is the path a 0 selects. Were the pin driven to 1 for a run, every sequence that calls `wait_fuse_sense_done` (40 sequence files under `cocotb/seq_lib/`; `smc_base_test_seq.py` defines the method and each leaf that needs the warm domain calls it itself) would time out on `tb_fuse_sense_done`, and every warm-domain CSR access would hang with no ready.
|Positive control: a leaf that drives the pin to 1 and requires `fuse_sense_done` to stay 0 while the shadow-register window still answers. It must stay in the cold domain: the eFuse controller sits under `rst_primary`, ahead of `rst_warm`, so its shadow reads through the eFuse CSR window -- over the OTP JTAG AXI-Lite port or the fabric path -- are the only thing that answers under bypass; nothing in the warm domain does. It needs a driven pin (a bench input in `smc_tb_signal_list.svh`, idle 0) in place of the constant. Closes when that leaf is enrolled, or when SEP-integrated signoff (SMU with SEP=1) is accepted as the only place the token-match path is exercised and that acceptance is recorded here.
|minshaoho (`9282d8c14`)
|2026-12-15
|===

[[smc-bench-standins]]
===== Bench stand-ins

A stand-in is DV-owned RTL that answers in place of a product cell or an
adopter block. Two sit on proof paths of this bench. Each has an inventory
row, a control row, and -- where the stand-in can supply a verdict -- one row
per enrolled leaf stating which crossing is on its proof path, what the
verdict reads, and the result of the control. `hw/sys/smc/dv/README.md`,
`tb/verilator_stubs/README.md` and `models/README.md` describe layout and
enrolment and point here; the dispositions live only here.

Kinds used in the leaf tables:

* *reads the crossing* -- the verdict samples the stand-in's output itself
  (a CSR field or a `tb_top` tap wired to `q_o`). On Verilator that value has
  the stand-in as its author until the pipe has filled.
* *downstream* -- the verdict reads product state that the DUT derives from
  the crossing's output.
* *upstream* -- the verdict reads the near side; the crossing carries the
  value away and nothing behind it is read.
* *none* -- no `prim_sync2` / `prim_sync3` is on the proof path. Listed
  because the audit's reset, clock and IRQ buckets signed off as a whole.

[cols="2,3,3,2",options="header"]
|===
|Stand-in |Replaces |Enrolled by |Tools it reaches
|`tb/verilator_stubs/prim_sync2.sv`, `prim_sync3.sv`
|`hw/common/och_prim/rtl/prim_sync2.sv` / `prim_sync3.sv`: `prim_flop_2sync` / `prim_flop_3sync` behind `prim_sync_randomized_delay`. `RANDOM_DELAY_ENABLE` is not defined on any DV build (`SYNTHESIS` is defined only on `[targets.dual]`), so `prim_sync_randomized_delay` passes its input through and the product cells are also a plain flop pipe; the one behaviour the stand-in adds is `initial '0` on every stage, a defined `0` on the far side before the pipe has filled where the product cell shows X on a four-state simulator.
|`smc_sim_cfg.toml` `[build].stubs`, emitted ahead of the Bender filelist so Verilator's `-Wno-MODDUP` first-definition-wins picks them. The product cells stay in the same compile: the generated Bender filelist `build/smc_bender.f` (`[build].bender_filelist`).
|Verilator and Xcelium compile them; VCS does not. The runner (`tools/dv/runlib/stages.py`) filters `[build].stubs` only when `tool == "vcs"`: there it drops any stub whose basename the Bender graph supplies, so the VCS compile list carries no `prim_sync2/3` stub and the product cells elaborate. For every other tool in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` `tools` -- Verilator and Xcelium -- it keeps both files and emits them ahead of the Bender filelist, so their compile lists carry the stub and the product cell together. Verilator's `-Wno-MODDUP` first-definition-wins selects the stub; no Xcelium build of this bench is recorded, so which of the pair Xcelium elaborates is not established by this record.
|`models/axil_okay_slv.sv` (behind `models/pll_wrap.sv` and `pvt_wrap.sv`)
|Nothing in the product RTL. It terminates the adopter PLL and PVT AXI-Lite windows that a real integration fills with the adopter's CSRs, completing every access with `RESP_OKAY` and all-zero read data. pulp `axi_err_slv` cannot hold that place: it asserts at time zero on every simulator but Verilator.
|Bender `smc_wrapper` target: `hw/top/smc_ip_integration.sv` `u_pll_wrap` / `u_pvt_wrap`.
|Every tool.
|===

[cols="2,4,1,1,2",options="header"]
|===
|Stand-in |Control that keeps the claims honest |Owner |Review |Closes when
|`prim_sync2/3`
|Height-3 control: the `reset`, `clock` and `irq` groups run on VCS with the product cells, `python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut smc --items reset clock irq --tool vcs --regress`, VCS X-2025.06_Full64, 35 leaves; the VCS compile list carries the product `prim_sync2.sv` / `prim_sync3.sv` from `smc_bender.f` and no stub. Run `20260914_022740__vcs__reset`, before the waiver: cocotb verdict 35 of 35 PASS with every `CHK-*` token of each card present; runner grade 35 of 35 FAIL, all on one message class -- the PeakRDL immediate assertions `assert_bad_ext_wr_ack` / `assert_bad_ext_rd_ack` in `cpu_ctrl_reg`, `reset_unit_reg` and `system_timer_octs_reg` (six sites) each report once `at time 0 fs`, before any reset, while `arst_n` and the ack strobes are still X; no leaf reports them at a later time. That class is waived in `smc_sim_cfg.toml` `[pass_fail]` in the form SEP uses for `abr_reg`: an ignore pattern naming exactly these six assertions together with the `at time 0 fs` text, paired with a hard-fail pattern that turns a later firing of the same assertions into an error. Run `20260914_035111__vcs__reset`, with the waiver committed and the tree clean (`result.json` `git.dirty = false`): cocotb 35 of 35 PASS, runner 35 of 35 PASS, the six time-0 lines recorded as `IGNORED` evidence in every leaf and no `Error:` line at any later time. One product assertion fires at a non-zero time and is not waived: `prim_ag_clk_mux.sv` `CheckMutualExclusionClk1` in `avsbus_controller.u_refclk_apbclk_mux` at 4688 ns in `smc_cg_dft_reset_bringup_test`, the DFT `test_en_i` bypass leaf, on seed 1611842931 (the control run's seed). The waived regression drew seed 1024727039 and did not reach it; a replay of the leaf on seed 1611842931 with the waiver in place (`20260914_035535__vcs__dft_seed1611842931`) fires it again and is graded FAIL on that line, so the firing is seed-dependent and the waiver does not cover it. Verilator compiles assertions out, so only a commercial run can see it. Neither observation depends on the synchroniser cells. The group selection excludes five entries of the three testlist files (named above the reset-group leaf table); run `20260914_031352__vcs__standin_files_minus_groups` covered those five on the same VCS build: cocotb 5 of 5 PASS, runner 5 of 5 PASS, no assertion after time 0.

====== prim_sync2 / prim_sync3 crossings in the DUT

`smc_peripherals` instantiates `smc_peripherals_cdc` with `SYNC_STAGES = 3`, so
its crossings are `prim_sync3`. Under `smc_wrapper` the stand-ins replace:

[cols="3,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Instance |Carries |Read by the bench through
|`u_smc_base.u_ext_interrupts_sync3` |`ext_interrupts_i` into `clk_smc`, then `cpu_interrupts_o` |`tb_ext_interrupt_0_sync` and `tb_temp_interrupt_irq` (both wired to the synchroniser output); the cluster PLIC
|`u_smc_base.u_ext_debug_bus_sync3` |`ext_debug_bus_i` |nothing: `tb_top` ties the input to `'0`
|`smc_subsystem_resets.u_reset_complete_sync` |`ss_reset_complete_i` (32 bits) |`SS_RESET_COMPLETE` CSR
|`smc_cool_reset_wrap.u_isolate_req_pin_sync`, `u_rst_cool_ni_sync_smc`, `u_rst_cool_no_sync_smc` |pin and reset visibility |`ISOLATE_REQ_VIS` CSR. The reset tree itself takes `rst_cool_ni` through the `clk_ref_i` de-glitch in `smc_reset_ctrl`, not through these.
|`smc_cool_reset_wrap.u_cfg_flr_pf_active_sync_smc`, `u_cfg_flr_pf_active_sync_ref` |`cfg_flr_pf_active_i` into both domains; the FLR edge detect behind them drives `rst_cool_no`, `isolate_req_o` and skip-mem-repair |`tb_isolate_req_o`, `tb_rst_cool_from_flr`, reset observables
|`smc_peripherals.u_boot_stall_combined_sync` |pad-57 / JTAG boot stall into `clk_smc`, then `boot_stall_sticky` and the fuse-reset gate |`tb_boot_stall_combined_o` (`boot_stall_processed_o`); whether the CPU fetches
|`smc_peripherals.u_rst_ext_wdt_irq_sync` |`sep_wdt_reset_n_i` as an interrupt, `peripheral_interrupts[26]` |IRQ aggregates only; the reset effect of the same pin enters the reset unit directly
|`smc_peripherals_cdc.u_ndmreset_request_sync` |`ndmreset_request_i` into `clk_smc`; NDM handshake and `peripheral_interrupts[11]` |`tb_ndmreset_irq`, `tb_ndmreset_process`, `NDMRESET_*` CSRs
|`smc_peripherals_cdc.u_i2c_enable_sync`, `u_i2c_irq_sync`, `u_i2c_debug_sync`, `u_uart_enable_sync`, `u_uart_combined_irq_sync`, `u_i3c_irq_sync`, `u_avsbus_irq_sync` |peripheral enables and interrupts from the peripheral clock into `clk_smc` |`tb_i2c0_enable`, `tb_i2c_irq`, `tb_avsbus_irq`, `peripheral_interrupts`. `tb_uart_irq_any` reads `uart_interrupt_o`, the peripheral-clock side, not the crossing.
|`smc_peripherals_cdc.u_i2c_cg_en_sync`, `u_uart_cg_en_sync`, `u_avs_cg_en_periph_sync`, `u_i3c_cg_en_sync`, `u_avs_cg_en_ref_sync`, `u_tel_cg_en_sync` |clock-gate enables out of `clk_smc` |not read: every `tb_*_cg_en` tap is the `clk_smc` side
|`avsbus_controller.u_gate_refclk_en_sync`, `u_idle_clk_reg_resync`, `u_slave_resync_pending_resync`, `u_readback_fifo_full_resync`, `u_slave_unresponsive_resync`, `u_slave_interrupt_resync`, `u_avs_readback_en_resync`, `u_max_retries_attempted_resync`; the gray-pointer `prim_sync3` arrays of `cmd_async_fifo_inst` and `readasync_back_fifo_inst` (`avsbus_async_fifo`) |AVSBus internal resynchronisation and the command / read-back FIFO pointers |AVSBus state and CSRs
|`smc_cpu_ctrl_wrap` `prim_refclk_count_w_cdc` (two `prim_sync3`) |`REFERENCE_COUNTER` handshake across `clk_ref_i` / `clk_smc` |`REFERENCE_COUNTER` CSR
|===

No eFuse leaf crosses one: the eFuse reset synchroniser is the vendored
`prim_rst_sync`, compiled from source on every tool. The pulp `sync` shim
(`hw/common/sync.sv`, Bender `common_cell_sync_shim`) reaches `prim_sync2r` /
`prim_sync3r` for every caller in this build, so the `axi_cdc` FIFOs in
`smc_peripherals_cdc` are not behind a stand-in.

====== Leaves: reset group

Control column: cocotb verdict on VCS with the product cells, then the
runner grade, then the run that measured it. Rows of leaves in the `reset`,
`clock` or `irq` group report the group control run
`20260914_022740__vcs__reset`, before the waiver: every runner FAIL below is
its time-0 external-ack class, none a checker of the leaf, and the waived
re-run `20260914_035111__vcs__reset` grades the same 35 leaves PASS. The three
files also hold five entries that belong to none of the three groups --
`smc_jtag_reset_proxy_test`, `smc_flr_sanity_test` and
`smc_multi_reset_csr_persistence_test` in `reset.toml`,
`smc_zeroer_dma_timeout_test` in `clock.toml`, `smc_mailbox_irq_test` in
`irq.toml` -- so a group selection never ran them. Their rows report the
separate run `20260914_031352__vcs__standin_files_minus_groups`: `--items` naming the five,
`--tool vcs --regress`, the waiver in place, VCS X-2025.06_Full64, the same
compile list (product cells from `smc_bender.f`, no stub). Cocotb 5 of 5 PASS,
runner 5 of 5 PASS, the six time-0 lines `IGNORED` in every leaf, no assertion
reported at a non-zero time. The reverse case is
`smc_irq_during_powergood_glitch_test`: an `irq` group member, so in the
control run, but defined in `combined.toml`.

====== axil_okay_slv

The six retired consumers, each `adopter-pending OUT` in the table above, in
no testlist, and never reporting PASS: `smc_pll_pvt_clock_config_test`,
`smc_pll_dvfs_depth_test`, `smc_pll_cgm_awm_config_test`,
`smc_pvt_analog_sensor_test`, `smc_pll_awm_freq_sweep_test`,
`smc_pvt_droop_test`. Each reads the PLL or PVT window and expects
`OKAY` with data `0`, which is the value the stand-in authors, so the
expected and the actual value have the same author and the compare holds on
any RTL behind it, including none. The catalog-only PLL names
(`smc_pll_init_test`, `smc_pll_fuse_init_code_test`, `smc_pll_dvfs_test`,
`smc_combined_pvt_sanity_test`, `smc_pll_disabled_gpio_test`) and
`smc_macro_axil_routing_test` would sit behind the same terminator.

[cols="3,3,4,2",options="header"]
|===
|Consumer |What it proves |What it does not prove |Control
|`prove_axil_external_active_probe` (`cocotb/seq_lib/smc_probe_positive_control.py`), the same-run positive control declared by `smc_canonical_smoke_test`, `smc_axil_idle_test`, `smc_axil_burst_idle_test` and `smc_6agent_observability_test` (`probe_positive_controls`)
|Reachability: a frontdoor SEP_IN read of the PLL window drives `u_smc.smc_external_req_o.ar_valid` high, `tb_axil_external_active` samples 1 during it and returns to 0 after, so the idle `== 0` compares the scoreboard books on that probe are fail-capable. Both levels are DUT-driven.
|Any register value. The `rdata == 0` the control reads back is the stand-in's `RESP_DATA` constant, not a DUT reset value, and no card cites it. `smc_axi_monitor.py` keeps PLL and PVT out of its DECERR window list for the same reason: the windows answer OKAY by construction.
|The probe itself: `hits > 0` and the return to idle are asserted, and the run fails if the pulse is never seen. `smc_scoreboard.py` also asserts the read-back against `expected = 0`, the stand-in's constant; that compare holds on any RTL behind the window, including none.
|===

[[smc-scheduled-holdouts]]
===== Leaves no scheduled tier runs

Every SMC leaf either runs on a scheduled tier -- `smoke` per PR (`sim.yml`),
`hosted` nightly and weekly (`regress.yml`) -- or has its hold-out recorded
here with the reason and the
closing condition. The hosted runners carry Verilator, bender and uv only.
The `ocah-toolchain` image (`tools/docker/Dockerfile`: `gcc-riscv64-unknown-elf`
and `picolibc-riscv64-unknown-elf`) is built locally and published to no
registry, and no workflow builds firmware, so every firmware leaf below, each
of which runs a `c_compile` stage, is run on demand (`scripts/docker-run.sh`
builds the images) until CI has a toolchain. The `smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test`
row is held out for a different reason: it fails the comparison it was written
to make, and waits on a ruling.

|`smc_occp_dual_unsecure_boot_test` |`occp_dual` |`target = "dual"`, `firmware = dual_occp` (three images), `timeout_sec = 21600`: hours of wall clock while a full sram image crosses a ~2.6 MHz serial bus |minshaoho (`9282d8c14`) |2026-12-15 |Toolchain as above and a runner budget that fits it: its own weekly `items: occp_dual` row with a timeout near the six-hour hosted cap
|`smc_cpu_mmio_read_wedge_test` |`cpu`, `all` |`firmware = "mmio_wedge"`: the wedge image is built with RISC-V GCC and picolibc, so no hosted-runner tier can run it. The two `smc_cpu_l2_*_wedge_test` leaves beside it in `cpu.toml` need no image and are enrolled in `hosted`; these two are not |minshaoho (`9282d8c14`) |2026-12-15 |Toolchain as above; the leaf then joins `hosted` alongside the L2 pair and this row closes
|`smc_cpu_mmio_write_wedge_test` |`cpu`, `all` |`firmware = "mmio_wedge"`, the same image and the same build path |minshaoho (`9282d8c14`) |2026-12-15 |As above
|`smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test` |none |In `testlists/holdout.toml` (`held_out`), outside `all`, run on demand. It fails the rate comparison it was written to make: `clk_rst.adoc:37-39` places the OCTS system timer in the reference clock domain, `smc_peripherals.sv:945-947` clocks `system_timer_octs` from `clk_smc_i`, and on seed 608337836 TIMER_COUNT advanced 992 counts while 428 `clk_ref_i` and 1026 `clk_smc_i` edges elapsed over the same window, against a tolerance of 14. It keeps its `[[tests]]` entry in `cpu.toml` and runs on demand (`--items smc_ref_clock_timer_rate_test`) |minshaoho (`9282d8c14`) |2026-12-15 |The clock-domain discrepancy is ruled: either `system_timer_octs` takes `clk_ref_i` and the leaf joins the `clock` group with the `ci` tag, or `clk_rst.adoc` is corrected and the leaf's expectation moves to `clk_smc_i`; if neither is ruled, the leaf joins `rtl_issue` under `expect_fail` with the rate assertion as `expect_fail_match`
|===

`dual.toml` is therefore in the toolchain bucket, not an "elaboration only"
bucket: all four of its leaves declare `firmware`. `dual` is the class group of the three leaves in `all`;
`--items occp_dual` selects the unsecure-boot leaf.

`rtl_issue` reproducers are not hold-outs. Such an entry carries
`expect_fail` and `expect_fail_match` (`hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/holdout.toml`),
the weekly tier runs the group on a `regress.yml` row of its own, which the
dashboard does not read, and the runner grades the recorded failure as PASS;
an observed PASS grades `expected_fail_passed`, the signal to move the leaf
into its feature testlist. The group has no member at present:
`smc_deadspace_decode_test` left it when #585 closed.
`smc_efuse_read_timeout_status_test` is not a member either: a read that hits
`EFUSE_READ_REQ_TIMEOUT` completes with `EFUSE_READ_CTRL.READ_STATUS` set, so
the leaf passes, proves `READ_STATUS` 0 -> 1 and runs from `efuse.toml` in
`all` and `hosted`.

[[smc-fw-disposition]]
==== SMC firmware image disposition

This is the disposition of record for every firmware image name under
`hw/sys/smc/dv/fw/tests/`. The shared engine (`hw/common/dv/fw/compile.mk`)
builds every `tests/<name>/` directory it finds, so being built is not a
consumer. A consumer is a testlist entry that stages the image: a
`firmware = "<name>"` key in `hw/sys/smc/dv/testlists/*.toml` resolved through
a `[c_build.*]` block of `smc_sim_cfg.toml`, or an SMU DV `[c_build.*]` that
builds and stages it. Every directory in the tree has a row here, and the rule
for adding one is in `hw/sys/smc/dv/fw/README.md`: a new image is added
together with its consumer and its row.

Buckets:

* `enrolled` -- staged by a testlist entry in this tree. The row names the
  leaf and the `[c_build.*]` block.
* `external-consumer` -- staged only by another package: an SMU DV group
  outside SMU `all`, or a regression outside this repository. The image is
  kept for that consumer and is not counted toward this tree's coverage.
* `deferred` -- kept for a stated blocker. The row is the blocker and the
  condition that closes it; a closed row moves to `enrolled` with its leaf,
  or to `superseded`.
* `superseded` -- the property is proven by a live enrolled name, or the image
  duplicates another image that has its own row, or the image has no DUT
  property. There is no directory in the tree; the code is in git history under
  the image name, and the row is what a reader searching for that name finds.

The cocotb test names of this package have their own record,
<<smc-deferred-disposition>>; rows below that cite a waived cocotb alias
point at it. SMU's record of the tests that stage SMC images is
`hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`.
